(dynamic workflow)","argumentHint":"","builtin":true},{"name":"design","description":"Grant or revoke Claude agent access to your Design projects","argumentHint":"consent | revoke","builtin":true},{"name":"design-sync","description":"Push a React design system to claude.ai/design. This runs a converter that bundles the real component code (from Storybook or a bare package) and uploads it. Use when the user runs /design-sync or says \"sync my design system to Claude Design\".","argumentHint":"[<project hint, e.g. \"Acme DS\">]","builtin":true},{"name":"dataviz","description":"Use this skill whenever you are about to create ANY chart, graph, plot, dashboard, or data visualization, in ANY output medium — an HTML or React artifact, inline SVG, plotting code in any library (matplotlib, plotly, d3, Recharts, …), an image/PNG you will render and upload, or a chart shared into Slack. Read it BEFORE writing the first line of chart code, choosing chart colors, building a stat tile / meter / KPI row, or laying out a dashboard. When the destination is a first-party document connector (host-designated, never self-described) that renders live charts, hand it the rows (inline, or as an uploaded data file the chart cites) rather than a rendered PNG/SVG — a picture of a chart loses hover, data inspection and per-value comments. Produces visualizations that read as one system — elegant, accessible, consistent in light and dark — using a brand-neutral placeholder palette you swap for your own. Teaches a design-system-agnostic method: a form heuristic, a color formula with a runnable validator, mark specs, and interaction rules. A validated default palette is documented in `references/palette.md` — swap that file's values for your brand's. Triggers on: \"chart\", \"graph\", \"plot\", \"data viz\", \"visualization\", \"dashboard\", \"analytics\", \"visualize data\", \"categorical colors\", \"sequential / diverging palette\", \"stat tile\", \"sparkline\", \"heatmap\", \"legend\", \"axis\", \"tooltip\", \"chart colors\", \"color by series\".","argumentHint":"","builtin":true},{"name":"update-config","description":"Use this skill to configure the Claude Code harness via settings.json. Automated behaviors (\"from now on when X\", \"each time X\", \"whenever X\", \"before/after X\") require hooks configured in settings.json - the harness executes these, not Claude, so memory/preferences cannot fulfill them. Also use for: permissions (\"allow X\", \"add permission\", \"move permission to\"), env vars (\"set X=Y\"), hook troubleshooting, or any changes to settings.json/settings.local.json files. Examples: \"allow npm commands\", \"add bq permission to global settings\", \"move permission to user settings\", \"set DEBUG=true\", \"when claude stops show X\". For simple settings like theme/model, suggest the /config command.","argumentHint":"","builtin":true},{"name":"verify","description":"Verify that a code change actually does what it's supposed to by exercising it end-to-end and observing behavior — drive the affected flow, not just tests or typecheck. Run before committing nontrivial changes; bootstraps this repo's project verify skill if none exists yet. Don't invoke it on a diff that only touches tests, docs, or other code with no runtime surface to drive (a change to product source always has one) — there's nothing to observe.","argumentHint":"","builtin":true},{"name":"debug","description":"Enable debug logging for this session and help diagnose issues","argumentHint":"[issue description]","builtin":true},{"name":"code-review","description":"Review the current diff, or a PR number/branch/path target, for correctness bugs (plus reuse/simplification/efficiency cleanups where the model's review recipe covers them) at the given effort level (low/medium: fewer, high-confidence findings; high→max: broader coverage, may include uncertain findings; ultra: deep multi-agent review in the cloud); with no level given, it reuses the level you typed last. Pass --comment to post findings as inline PR comments, or --fix to apply the findings to the working tree after the review. For ultra on a GitHub.com PR target, --post asks to post the finished review’s findings to the PR as a single comment from the user’s GitHub account (not a review; the launch dialog still confirms in interactive sessions, while non-interactive mode posts on the flag alone) and --no-post hides that option.","argumentHint":"[low|medium|high|xhigh|max|ultra] [--fix] [--comment] [<pr#>|<branch>|<path>]","aliases":["review"],"builtin":true},{"name":"simplify","description":"Review the changed code for reuse, simplification, efficiency, and altitude cleanups, then apply the fixes. Quality only — it does not hunt for bugs; use /code-review for that.","argumentHint":"[<target>]","builtin":true},{"name":"batch","description":"Research and plan a large-scale change, then execute it in parallel across 5–30 isolated worktree agents that each open a PR.","argumentHint":"<instruction>","builtin":true},{"name":"fewer-permission-prompts","description":"Scan your transcripts for common read-only Bash and MCP tool calls, then add a prioritized allowlist to project .claude/settings.json to reduce permission prompts.","argumentHint":"","builtin":true},{"name":"doctor","description":"Health-check the user's Claude Code setup and fix issues: diagnose installation health — what the `claude doctor` terminal diagnostics cover — from local data (duplicate or leftover installs, PATH, unparseable settings files, broken or colliding agent definitions, skills whose frontmatter fails to parse); find unused skills, MCP servers, and plugins versus their context cost and disable dead weight; deduplicate local CLAUDE.md files against checked-in ones; trim checked-in CLAUDE.md files by cutting content a session could derive from the codebase (directory layouts, tech-stack lists, architecture overviews) while keeping gotchas, rationale, and non-standard conventions; migrate always-loaded CLAUDE.md guidance into lazy skills and nested CLAUDE.md files; flag slow hooks and context-heavy extensions; check the installed version is current; make auto mode the default permission mode; and pre-approve frequently denied read-only commands. Use when the user asks for a doctor run, checkup, audit, tune-up, or cleanup of their Claude Code setup or configuration.","argumentHint":"","aliases":["checkup"],"builtin":true},{"name":"loop","description":"Run a prompt or slash command on a recurring interval (e.g. /loop 5m /foo). Claude desktop app (also called the in-app browser, the browser pane, Claude's browser, or \"your own browser\"), whose tools are named mcp__Claude_Browser__* when the session runs in the desktop app and mcp__remote-devices__Claude_Browser__* when a cloud session is linked to the person's computer; before those tools are turned on there may be a single enable__mcp__remote-devices__Claude_Browser tool instead. It covers the pane's persistent sign-ins, tabs and preview_start, reading pages as text, site approvals, what the pane cannot open, and what to do when it cannot be reached. It is not for Claude in Chrome (mcp__claude-in-chrome__* tools), which has its own skill, and it does not decide which browser to use. (claude.ai sync)","argumentHint":"","aliases":["built-in-browser"]},{"name":"anthropic-skills:chrome-browser","description":"Read this skill before the first step that uses Claude in Chrome, the browser extension whose tools are named mcp__claude-in-chrome__* (also called Chrome, the browser extension, or the external browser) and which acts in the person's real Chrome with their own sign-ins; before those tools are turned on there may be a single enable__mcp__claude-in-chrome tool instead. It covers loading the tools in one ToolSearch call, checking the person's open tabs and working in a new tab, site permissions, GIF recordings, console logs, dialogs to avoid, and when to stop and ask. It is not for the built-in browser (mcp__Claude_Browser__* or mcp__remote-devices__Claude_Browser__* tools), which has its own skill, and it does not decide which browser to use. (claude.ai sync)","argumentHint":"","aliases":["chrome-browser"]},{"name":"anthropic-skills:computer-use","description":"Read this skill before the first step of any request to do something in an app on the person's own computer (Notes, Finder, System Settings, any desktop app), to look at their screen, or for \"computer use\". Computer use (desktop control) lets Claude take screenshots of the person's desktop and control it with clicks, typing and scrolling through the Claude desktop app; its tools are named mcp__computer-use__* when the session runs in the desktop app and mcp__remote-devices__computer_* when a cloud session is linked to the person's computer; before computer use is turned on for a conversation there may be no such tools, only an enable__mcp__remote-devices__computer tool, which turns it on. It covers turning it on, picking the right tool, the access flow, and the safety rules for tiered apps, links and financial actions. It is not for websites, which go through Claude in Chrome or the built-in browser and their own skills. (claude.ai sync)","argumentHint":"","aliases":["computer-use"]},{"name":"anthropic-skills:deep-research","description":"Use this skill when the user's prompt requires (1) researching a topic across multiple sources, comparing options or alternatives, analyzing trends or history, understanding markets or industries, or reviewing literature or studies and (2) synthesizing that research into a comprehensive, narrative report. If you're planning to search the web or internal knowledge bases, consider using this skill. This skill coordinates research subagents, so use it only when you have a tool for spawning subagents (the Agent or Task tool); otherwise, research the question directly. (claude.ai sync)","argumentHint":""},{"name":"anthropic-skills:docs","description":"docs (living docs people share, comment on and edit; use only when the user asks for one: names a doc, document, page, memo, spec, PRD, runbook or write-up, asks for somewhere to share or keep editing something, or says yes to your doc offer; a plan, comparison, summary or notes asked in chat stays in chat (at most a one-line doc offer); a report, status update, recap or \"something I can send them\" with no form named → ask first: reply, doc or file?; tabs hold tables and live charts too; a pasted claude.ai/code/artifact/… link may be a doc: check with docs tools first; not HTML pages, apps or plain chat answers; a .docx/.pptx/.xlsx/PDF asked for by name → that format's skill): asked for one → no docs-connector instructions in context? call the docs connector's `guide` with topic.instructions first, then create the doc (headings only, no body) before any search, file read or plan, even with files attached. Documenting code means docstrings or repo docs, not a doc. (claude.ai sync)","argumentHint":"","aliases":["docs"]},{"name":"anthropic-skills:docx","description":"Use this skill whenever the user wants to create, read, edit, or manipulate Word documents (.docx) or Word templates (.dotx). Triggers include: any mention of 'Word doc', 'word document', '.docx', '.dotx', or requests to produce professional documents with formatting like tables of contents, page numbers, or letterheads. Also use when extracting or reorganizing content from .docx or .dotx files, inserting or replacing images in documents, find-and-replace in Word files, working with tracked changes or comments, or converting content into a polished Word document. If the user asks for a 'report', 'memo', 'letter', 'template', or similar deliverable as a Word or .docx file (to download, email or print), use this skill. However, if they ask for a document, page, report, memo, or notes WITHOUT naming a file format and the session offers Claude's own dedicated document or page skill or connector, use that instead. Do NOT use for PDFs, spreadsheets, Google Docs, or coding unrelated to document generation. (claude.ai sync)","argumentHint":"","aliases":["docx"]},{"name":"anthropic-skills:import-memory","description":"Import a memory export from another AI assistant into Claude's memory — conversationally, additively, and with the content treated as data. (claude.ai sync)","argumentHint":"","aliases":["import-memory"]},{"name":"anthropic-skills:morning","description":"Render the user's morning brief as a styled HTML artifact, or set it up as a recurring weekday task. Use only when the user explicitly asks to run, see, or set up their morning brief, or if they invoke /morning by name. A question about their day, schedule, or calendar is not by itself a request for the brief; answer it directly instead. (claude.ai sync)","argumentHint":"","aliases":["morning"]},{"name":"anthropic-skills:pdf","description":"Use this skill whenever the user wants to do anything with PDF files. This includes reading or extracting text/tables from PDFs, combining or merging multiple PDFs into one, splitting PDFs apart, rotating pages, adding watermarks, creating new PDFs, filling PDF forms, encrypting/decrypting PDFs, extracting images, and OCR on scanned PDFs to make them searchable. If the user mentions a .pdf file or asks to produce one, use this skill. (claude.ai sync)","argumentHint":"","aliases":["pdf"]},{"name":"anthropic-skills:pptx","description":"Use this skill any time a .pptx or .potx file is involved in any way — as input, output, or both. This includes: creating slide decks, pitch decks, or presentations as PowerPoint (.pptx) files; reading, parsing, or extracting text from any .pptx or .potx file (even if the extracted content will be used elsewhere, like in an email, summary, or creating a different type of slide deck); editing, modifying, or updating existing presentations; combining or splitting slide files; working with templates (.potx), layouts, speaker notes, or comments. Trigger whenever the user asks for a PowerPoint or .pptx file, or references a .pptx or .potx filename, regardless of what they plan to do with the content afterward. However, when the user asks for a deck, slides, a slide deck, or a presentation without naming a file format, default to using a dedicated slide-deck artifact type or a separate slides skill if this session offers one; otherwise, use this skill. (claude.ai sync)","argumentHint":"","aliases":["pptx"]},{"name":"anthropic-skills:setup-writing-style","description":"Learns how the user writes from their own sent messages and docs, and builds a voice profile so future drafts sound like them instead of generic AI. The profile is saved as the my-writing-style skill. Use when the user asks to set up, learn, or capture their writing voice, or complains that drafts sound generic or unlike them and no my-writing-style profile exists. Only for drafting text the user will send as themselves, not for Claude's own replies. (claude.ai sync)","argumentHint":"","aliases":["setup-writing-style"]},{"name":"anthropic-skills:skill-creator","description":"Create new skills, modify and improve existing skills, and measure skill performance. Use when users want to create a skill from scratch, edit, or optimize an existing skill, run evals to test a skill, benchmark skill performance with variance analysis, or optimize a skill's description for better triggering accuracy. (claude.ai sync)","argumentHint":"","aliases":["skill-creator"]},{"name":"anthropic-skills:xlsx","description":"Use this skill any time a spreadsheet file is the primary input or output. This means any task where the user wants to: open, read, edit, or fix an existing .xlsx, .xlsm, .xltx, .csv, or .tsv file (e.g., adding columns, computing formulas, formatting, charting, cleaning messy data); create a new spreadsheet from scratch or from other data sources; or convert between tabular file formats. Trigger especially when the user references a spreadsheet file by name or path — even casually (like \"the xlsx in my downloads\") — and wants something done to it or produced from it. Also trigger for cleaning or restructuring messy tabular data files (malformed rows, misplaced headers, junk data) into proper spreadsheets. The deliverable must be a spreadsheet file. Do NOT trigger when the primary deliverable is a Word document, HTML report, standalone Python script, database pipeline, or Google Sheets API integration, even if tabular data is involved. (claude.ai sync)","argumentHint":"","aliases":["xlsx"]}],"uuid":"cf326ea4-90cd-447e-a48d-7183a88a89cf","session_id":"1e3ae1b1-9b85-4c9f-90ed-5bd54ae028a9"}

1 : 0;\n149\t    weakMatchCount += weakMatchedTests.length > 0 ? 1 : 0;\n150\t    const allowlisted = allowlist.has(source.relativePath);\n151\t    result.observations.push({\n152\t      id: `test-gap-source-file:${source.relativePath}`,\n153\t      type: 'test_gap_source_file',\n154\t      path: source.relativePath,\n155\t      line: 1,\n156\t      name: basename(source.relativePath),\n157\t      details: {\n158\t        riskClass: risk.riskClass,\n159\t        highRisk: risk.highRisk,\n160\t        securitySensitive: risk.securitySensitive,\n161\t        migrationHazard: risk.migrationHazard,\n162\t        writeBoundary: risk.writeBoundary,\n163\t        matchedTests: matchedTests.map((test) => test.relativePath).sort(),\n164\t        weakMatchedTests: weakMatchedTests.map((test) => test.relativePath).sort(),\n165\t        allowlisted,\n166\t      },\n167\t    });\n168\t    if (allowlisted || matchedTests.length > 0) {\n169\t      continue;\n170\t    }\n171\t    if (risk.migrationHazard) {\n172\t      highRiskWithoutTest += 1;\n173\t      result.findings.push({\n174\t        id: `migration-without-test:${source.relativePath}`,\n175\t        rule: 'migration_without_test',\n176\t        severity: 'high',\n177\t        path: source.relativePath,\n178\t        message: 'Hazardous migration has no adjacent or importing test coverage signal.',\n179\t        evidence: [{ path: source.relativePath }],\n180\t        confidence: weakMatchedTests.length > 0 ? 'review_required' : 'actionable',\n182\t        review_reason: weakMatchedTests.length > 0 ? 'weak_symbol_test_match' : undefined,\n183\t        details: { riskClass: risk.riskClass, weakMatchedTests: weakMatchedTests.map((test) => test.relativePath).sort() },\n184\t      });\n185\t      continue;\n186\t    }\n187\t    if (risk.securitySensitive) {\n188\t      highRiskWithoutTest += 1;\n189\t      result.findings.push({\n190\t        id: `security-source-without-test:${source.relativePath}`,\n191\t        rule: 'security_source_without_security_test',\n192\t        severity: 'high',\n193\t        path: source.relativePath,\n194\t        message: 'Security-sensitive source file has no adjacent or importing test coverage signal.',\n195\t        evidence: [{ path: source.relativePath }],\n196\t        confidence: weakMatchedTests.length > 0 ? 0.55 : 0.86,\n197\t        actionability: weakMatchedTests.length > 0 ? 'review_required' : 'actionable',\n198\t        review_reason: weakMatchedTests.length > 0 ? 'weak_symbol_test_match' : undefined,\n199\t        details: { riskClass: risk.riskClass, weakMatchedTests: weakMatchedTests.map((test) => test.relativePath).sort() },\n200\t      });\n201\t      continue;\n202\t    }\n203\t    if (input.includeWriteBoundaryFindings === true && risk.highRisk && risk.writeBoundary) {\n204\t      highRiskWithoutTest += 1;\n205\t      result.findings.push({\n206\t        id: `high-risk-source-without-adjacent-test:${source.relativePath}`,\n207\t        rule: 'high_risk_source_without_adjacent_test',\n208\t        severity: 'medium',\n209\t        path: source.relativePath,\n210\t        message: 'High-risk source file has no adjacent, sibling __tests__, or importing test coverage signal.',\n211\t        evidence: [{ path: source.relativePath }],\n212\t        confidence: weakMatchedTests.length > 0 ? 0.5 : 0.75,\n213\t        actionability: weakMatchedTests.length > 0 ? 'review_required' : 'actionable',\n214\t        review_reason: weakMatchedTests.length > 0 ? 'weak_symbol_test_match' : undefined,\n215\t        details: { riskClass: risk.riskClass, weakMatchedTests: weakMatchedTests.map((test) => test.relativePath).sort() },\n216\t      });\n217\t    }\n218\t  }\n219\t\n220\t  result.observations.push({\n221\t    id: 'test-gap-coverage-summary',\n222\t    type: 'test_gap_coverage_summary',\n223\t    details: {\n224\t      sourceFiles: sources.length,\n225\t      testFiles: tests.length,\n226\t      directMatchCount,\n227\t      weakMatchCount,\n228\t      highRiskWithoutTest,\n229\t      unmatchedHighRiskCount: highRiskWithoutTest,\n230\t      scanMode: 'import_graph_v1',\n231\t    },\n232\t  });\n233\t\n234\t  result.observations.sort(compareById);\n235\t  result.findings.sort(compareById);\n236\t  result.readPaths.sort();\n237\t  const evidenceSources = Array.from(\n238\t    new Set(result.findings.flatMap((finding) => finding.evidence.map((evidence) => evidence.path))),\n239\t /dangerouslySetInnerHTML|DOMPurify|sanitizeHtml|@Public\\b|AuthGuard|TenantGuard|PermissionGuard/i.test(text)\n272\t    || /(\\.guard|\\.strategy|\\/auth\\/|\\/security\\/|\\/permission)/i.test(path);\n273\t  const writeBoundary = /@(Post|Put|Patch|Delete|Mutation)\\b|\\b(create|update|delete|remove|archive|approve|reject|reset|invite)\\w*\\s*\\(/i.test(text);\n274\t  const migrationHazard = /\\/migrations\\/.*\\.ts$/.test(path)\n275\t    && /\\b(DROP|DELETE|TRUNCATE|ALTER\\s+TYPE|ENABLE\\s+ROW\\s+LEVEL\\s+SECURITY|FORCE\\s+ROW\\s+LEVEL\\s+SECURITY)\\b/i.test(text);\n276\t  return { riskClass, highRisk, securitySensitive, migrationHazard, writeBoundary };\n277\t}\n278\t\n279\tfunction riskClassForPath(path: string): string {\n280\t  if (path.includes('/migrations/')) {\n281\t    return 'migration';\n282\t  }\n283\t  // match[1] is narrowed explicitly so the file stays clean under\n284\t  // --noUncheckedIndexedAccess (the capture group is structurally guaranteed,\n285\t  // but the index signature cannot prove it to the compiler).\n286\t  const match = path.match(/\\.([a-z-]+)\\.(ts|tsx)$/);\n287\t  if (match?.[1] !== undefined) {\n288\t    return match[1];\n289\t  }\n290\t  if (path.endsWith('.tsx')) {\n291\t    return 'ui_component';\n292\t  }\n293\t  return 'source';\n294\t}\n295\t\n296\tfunction matchingTests(\n297\t  source: FileUnit,\n298\t  tests: basenameWithoutKnownSuffix(source.relativePath);\n303\t  const sourceDir = dirname(source.relativePath);\n304\t  return tests.filter((test) => {\n305\t    const testBase = basenameWithoutKnownSuffix(test.relativePath);\n306\t    if (testBase === sourceBase) {\n307\t      return true;\n308\t    }\n309\t    if (dirname(test.relativePath) === sourceDir && testBase.startsWith(sourceBase)) {\n310\t      return true;\n311\t    }\n312\t    if (dirname(test.relativePath).endsWith(`${sourceDir}/__tests__`) && testBase.includes(sourceBase)) {\n313\t      return true;\n314\t    }\n315\t    return importedSpecifiers(test.sourceFile).some((specifier) => {\n316\t      const resolved = resolveImportSpecifier(test.relativePath, specifier, sourcePaths, specifier.slice(alias.prefix.length, specifier.length - alias.suffix.length);\n362\t      for (const target of alias.targets) {\n363\t        candidates.push(target.replace('*', middle));\n364\t      }\n365\t    }\n366\t  }\n367\t  for (const candidate of candidates.flatMap(candidatePathVariants)) {\n368\t    if (sourcePaths.has(candidate)) {\n369\t      return candidate;\n370\t    }\n371\t  }\n372\t  return undefined;\n373\t}\n374\t\n375\tfunction candidatePathVariants(candidate: string): string[] {\n376\t  const normalized = normalizePath(candidate).replace(/^\\.\\//, '');\n377\t  return [\n378\t    normalized,\n379\t    `${normalized}.ts`,\n380\t    `${normalized}.tsx`,\n381\t    `${normalized}/index.ts`,\n382\t    `${normalized}/index.tsx`,\n383\t  ];\n384\t}\n385\t\n386\tfunction readPathAliases(workspaceRoot: string): readonly PathAlias[] {\n387\t  const path = resolve(workspaceRoot, 'tsconfig.base.json');\n388\t  if readonly string[]> };\n394\t    };\n395\t    return Object.entries(parsed.compilerOptions?.paths ?? {}).map(([key, targets]) => {\n396\t      const starIndex = key.indexOf('*');\n397\t      return {\n398\t        prefix: starIndex >= 0 ? key.slice(0, starIndex) : key,\n399\t        suffix: starIndex >= 0 ? key.slice(starIndex + 1) : '',\n400\t        targets: targets.map((target) => normalizePath(target)),\n401\t      };\n402\t    });\n403\t  } catch {\n404\t    return [];\n405\t  }\n406\t}\n407\t\n408\tfunction exportedSymbols(sourceFile: ts.SourceFile): string[] {\n409\t  const symbols = new Set<string>();\n410\t  visit(sourceFile, (node) => {\n411\t    if ((ts.isClassDeclaration(node) || ts.isFunctionDeclaration(node) || ts.isInterfaceDeclaration(node) || ts.isTypeAliasDeclaration(node)) && node.name) {\n412\t      const modifiers = ts.canHaveModifiers(node) ? ts.getModifiers(node) ?? [] : [];\n413\t      if (modifiers.some((modifier) => modifier.kind === ts.SyntaxKind.ExportKeyword)) {\n414\t        symbols.add(node.name.text);\n415\t      }\n416\t    }\n417\t  });\n418\t  return Array.from(symbols).sort();\n419\t}\n420\t\n421\tfunction readFileUnit(path: string, workspaceRoot: string): FileUnit {\n422\t  const text = readWorkspaceFile(path);\n423\t  return {\n424\t    path,\n425\t    relativePath: normalizePath(relative(workspaceRoot, path)),\n426\t    text,\n427\t    sourceFile: ts.createSourceFile(path, text, ts.ScriptTarget.Latest, true),\n428\t    isTest: isTestFile(path),\n429\t  };\n430\t}\n431\t\n432\tfunction collectSourceAndTestFiles(root: string): readonly string[] {\n433\t  return collectFiles(root, {\n434\t    extensions: ['.ts', '.tsx'],\n435\t    // E13 FP class (2): archived corpus (e.g. migrations/.archive/<timestamp>/)\n436\t    // is dead code — it can neither need test coverage nor PROVIDE it (an\n437\t    // archived spec must not satisfy a live source), so it is excluded from\n438\t    // the scan entirely rather than special-cased at finding time.\n439\t    includeFile: (name, path) => !name.endsWith('.d.ts') && !isArchivedWorkspacePath(path),\n440\t  });\n441\t}\n442\t\n443\tfunction isTestFile(path: string): boolean {\n444\t  const normalized = normalizePath(path);\n445\t  return /(__tests__|\\.spec\\.|\\.test\\.)/.test(normalized);\n446\t}\n447\t\n448\tfunction resolveInsideWorkspace(workspaceRoot: string, requestedPath: string): string {\n449\t  return resolveAdapterPath(workspaceRoot, requestedPath);\n450\t}\n451\t\n452\tfunction visit(node: ts.Node, visitor: (node: ts.Node) => void): void {\n453\t  visitor(node);\n454\t  ts.forEachChild(node, (child) => visit(child, visitor));\n455\t}\n456\t\n457\tfunction compareById(a: { readonly id: string }, b: { readonly id: string }): number {\n458\t  return a.id.localeCompare(b.id);\n459\t}\n460\t\n461\tfunction normalizePath(path: string): string {\n462\t  return normalizeWorkspacePath(path);\n463\t}\n464\t\n465\tfunction readStdin(): Promise<string> {\n466\t  return new Promise((resolvePromise, reject) => {\n467\t    let input = '';\n468\t    process.stdin.setEncoding('utf8');\n469\t    // setEncoding('utf8') makes every chunk a string at runtime, but the\n470\t    // stream's declared chunk type stays `string | Buffer` — concatenating the\n471\t    // union is what the type checker rejects. Narrow at the boundary rather\n472\t    // than widening `input` (kernel-dead-wire-adapter is the converged shape).\n473\t    process.stdin.on('data', (chunk: string | Buffer) => {\n474\t      input += typeof chunk === 'string' ? chunk : chunk.toString('utf8');\n475\t    });\n476\t    process.stdin.on('end', () => resolvePromise(input));\n477\t    process.stdin.on('error', reject);\n478\t  });\n479\t}\n480\t\n481\tasync function main(): Promise<void> {\n482\t  const rawInput = await readStdin();\n483\t  const input = rawInput.trim().length > 0 ? (JSON.parse(rawInput) as AdapterInput) : {};\n484\t  process.stdout.write(`${JSON.stringify(analyzeTestGaps(input))}\\n`);\n485\t}\n486\t\n487\tif (require.main === module) {\n488\t  main().catch((error: unknown) => {\n489\t    const message = error instanceof Error ? error.message : String(error);\n490\t    process.stderr.write(`${message}\\n`);\n491\t    process.exit(1);\n492\t  });\n493\t}\n494\t"}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"1e3ae1b1-9b85-4c9f-90ed-5bd54ae028a9","uuid":"bdb4aeb2-6912-4b2b-b380-72e4cef7a6ba","timestamp":"2026-09-19T23:18:47.226Z","tool_use_result":{"type":"text","file":{"filePath":"/home/gharunner/actions-runner/_work/aquaculture_platform/aquaculture_platform/aria-worktrees/req-AIR-aria-evidence-judge-0823b429a9e6/tools/aria-adapters/test-gap-adapter.ts","content":"#!/usr/bin/env readonly string[];\n}\n\n\nexport function analyzeTestGaps(input: AdapterInput, workspaceRoot = process.cwd()): AriaOutput {\n  const roots = requireScanRoots('test-gap-adapter', input.roots);\n  const allowlist = new Set((input.allowlist ?? []).map(normalizePath));\n  const files = roots\n    .map((root) => resolveInsideWorkspace(workspaceRoot, root))\n    .filter((root) => workspacePathExists(root))\n    .flatMap((root) => collectSourceAndTestFiles(root));\n  const snapshotFiles = filterFilesBySnapshot(files, workspaceRoot, input);\n  const units = snapshotFiles.map((file) => readFileUnit(file, workspaceRoot));\n  const tests = units.filter((unit) => unit.isTest);\n  const sources = units.filter((unit) => !unit.isTest);\n  const sourcePaths = new Set(sources.map((source) => source.relativePath));\n  const pathAliases = readPathAliases(workspaceRoot);\n  const result: AnalysisResult = {\n    observations: [],\n    findings: [],\n    readPaths: units.map((unit) => unit.relativePath),\n  };\n\n  for (const test of tests) {\n    result.observations.push({\n      id: `test-gap-test-file:${test.relativePath}`,\n      type: 'test_gap_test_file',\n      path: test.relativePath,\n      line: 1,\n      name: basename(test.relativePath),\n      details: {\n        imports: importedSpecifiers(test.sourceFile).sort(),\n      },\n    });\n  }\n\n  let highRiskWithoutTest = 0;\n  let directMatchCount = 0;\n  let weakMatchCount = 0;\n  for (const source of sources) {\n    const risk = classifySourceRisk(source);\n    if (!risk.highRisk && !risk.securitySensitive && !risk.migrationHazard) {\n      continue;\n    }\n    const matchedTests = matchingTests(source, tests, sourcePaths, pathAliases);\n    const weakMatchedTests = weakSymbolTests(source, tests).filter(\n      (test) => !matchedTests.some((matched) => matched.relativePath === test.relativePath),\n    );\n    directMatchCount += matchedTests.length > 0 ? 1 : 0;\n    weakMatchCount += weakMatchedTests.length > 0 ? 1 : 0;\n    const allowlisted = allowlist.has(source.relativePath);\n    result.observations.push({\n      id: `test-gap-source-file:${source.relativePath}`,\n      type: 'test_gap_source_file',\n      path: source.relativePath,\n      line: 1,\n      name: basename(source.relativePath),\n      details: {\n        riskClass: risk.riskClass,\n        highRisk: risk.highRisk,\n        securitySensitive: risk.securitySensitive,\n        migrationHazard: risk.migrationHazard,\n        writeBoundary: risk.writeBoundary,\n        matchedTests: matchedTests.map((test) => test.relativePath).sort(),\n        weakMatchedTests: weakMatchedTests.map((test) => test.relativePath).sort(),\n        allowlisted,\n      },\n    });\n    if (allowlisted || matchedTests.length > 0) {\n      continue;\n    }\n    if (risk.migrationHazard) {\n      highRiskWithoutTest += 1;\n      result.findings.push({\n        id: `migration-without-test:${source.relativePath}`,\n        rule: 'migration_without_test',\n        severity: 'high',\n        path: source.relativePath,\n        message: 'Hazardous migration has no adjacent or importing test coverage signal.',\n        evidence: [{ path: source.relativePath }],\n        confidence: weakMatchedTests.length > 0 ? 0.55 : 0.88,\n        actionability: weakMatchedTests.length > 0 ? 'review_required' : 'actionable',\n        review_reason: weakMatchedTests.length > 0 ? 'weak_symbol_test_match' : undefined,\n        details: { riskClass: risk.riskClass, weakMatchedTests: weakMatchedTests.map((test) => test.relativePath).sort() },\n      });\n      continue;\n    }\n    if (risk.securitySensitive) {\n      highRiskWithoutTest += 1;\n      result.findings.push({\n        id: `security-source-without-test:${source.relativePath}`,\n        rule: 'security_source_without_security_test',\n        severity: 'high',\n        path: source.relativePath,\n        message: 'Security-sensitive source file has no adjacent or importing test coverage signal.',\n        evidence: [{ path: source.relativePath }],\n        confidence: weakMatchedTests.length > 0 ? 0.55 : 0.86,\n        actionability: weakMatchedTests.length > 0 ? 'review_required' : 'actionable',\n        review_reason: weakMatchedTests.length > 0 ? 'weak_symbol_test_match' : undefined,\n        details: { riskClass: risk.riskClass, weakMatchedTests: weakMatchedTests.map((test) => test.relativePath).sort() },\n      });\n      continue;\n    }\n    if (input.includeWriteBoundaryFindings === true && risk.highRisk && risk.writeBoundary) {\n      highRiskWithoutTest += 1;\n      result.findings.push({\n        id: `high-risk-source-without-adjacent-test:${source.relativePath}`,\n        rule: 'high_risk_source_without_adjacent_test',\n        severity: 'medium',\n        path: source.relativePath,\n        message: 'High-risk source file has no adjacent, sibling __tests__, or importing test coverage signal.',\n        evidence: [{ path: source.relativePath }],\n        confidence: weakMatchedTests.length > 0 ? 0.5 : 0.75,\n        actionability: weakMatchedTests.length > 0 ? 'review_required' : 'actionable',\n        review_reason: weakMatchedTests.length > 0 ? 'weak_symbol_test_match' : undefined,\n        details: { riskClass: risk.riskClass, weakMatchedTests: weakMatchedTests.map((test) => test.relativePath).sort() },\n      });\n    }\n  }\n\n  result.observations.push({\n    id: 'test-gap-coverage-summary',\n    type: 'test_gap_coverage_summary',\n    details: {\n      sourceFiles: sources.length,\n      testFiles: tests.length,\n      directMatchCount,\n      weakMatchCount,\n      highRiskWithoutTest,\n      unmatchedHighRiskCount: highRiskWithoutTest,\n      scanMode: 'import_graph_v1',\n    },\n  });\n\n  result.observations.sort(compareById);\n  result.findings.sort(compareById);\n  result.readPaths.sort();\n  const evidenceSources = Array.from(\n    new Set(result.findings.flatMap((finding) => finding.evidence.map((evidence) => evidence.path))),\n  ).sort();\n\n  return {\n    observations: result.observations,\n    findings: result.findings,\n    read_paths: Array.from(new Set(result.readPaths)).sort(),\n    evidence_sources: evidenceSources,\n    belief_candidates: [\n      {\n        belief_id: 'test-gap:high-risk-source-coverage-surface',\n        claim: 'high-risk backend, web security, and migration surfaces can be mapped to adjacent or importing test coverage signals',\n        confidence: 0.75,\n        evidence_refs: ['apps/**/*.ts', 'libs/**/*.ts', 'platform/libs/**/*.ts', 'web/**/*.tsx'],\n        source_tool_id: 'test-gap-adapter',\n      },\n    ],\n    cost_units: Array.from(new Set(result.readPaths)).length,\n    metadata: {\n      adapter: 'test-gap-adapter',\n      roots: roots.map(String).sort(),\n      files_scanned: snapshotFiles.length,\n      findings_count: result.findings.length,\n      allowlist_count: allowlist.size,\n    },\n  };\n}\n\nfunction classifySourceRisk(unit: FileUnit): SourceRisk {\n  const path = unit.relativePath;\n  const text = unit.text;\n  const riskClass = riskClassForPath(path);\n  const highRisk = /(\\.controller|\\.resolver|\\.handler|\\.guard|\\.strategy|\\.middleware|\\.interceptor)\\.ts$/.test(path);\n  const securitySensitive = /dangerouslySetInnerHTML|DOMPurify|sanitizeHtml|@Public\\b|AuthGuard|TenantGuard|PermissionGuard/i.test(text)\n    || /(\\.guard|\\.strategy|\\/auth\\/|\\/security\\/|\\/permission)/i.test(path);\n  const writeBoundary = /@(Post|Put|Patch|Delete|Mutation)\\b|\\b(create|update|delete|remove|archive|approve|reject|reset|invite)\\w*\\s*\\(/i.test(text);\n  const migrationHazard = /\\/migrations\\/.*\\.ts$/.test(path)\n    && /\\b(DROP|DELETE|TRUNCATE|ALTER\\s+TYPE|ENABLE\\s+ROW\\s+LEVEL\\s+SECURITY|FORCE\\s+ROW\\s+LEVEL\\s+SECURITY)\\b/i.test(text);\n  return { riskClass, highRisk, securitySensitive, migrationHazard, writeBoundary };\n}\n\nfunction riskClassForPath(path: string): string {\n  if (path.includes('/migrations/')) {\n    return 'migration';\n  }\n  // match[1] is narrowed explicitly so the file stays clean under\n  // --noUncheckedIndexedAccess (the capture group is structurally guaranteed,\n  // but the index signature cannot prove it to the compiler).\n  const match = path.match(/\\.([a-z-]+)\\.(ts|tsx)$/);\n  if (match?.[1] !== undefined) {\n    return match[1];\n  }\n  if (path.endsWith('.tsx')) {\n    return 'ui_component';\n  }\n  return 'source';\n}\n\nfunction matchingTests(\n  source: FileUnit,\n  tests: readonly FileUnit[],\n  sourcePaths: ReadonlySet<string>,\n  pathAliases: readonly PathAlias[],\n): readonly FileUnit[] {\n  const sourceBase = basenameWithoutKnownSuffix(source.relativePath);\n  const sourceDir = dirname(source.relativePath);\n  return tests.filter((test) => {\n    const testBase = basenameWithoutKnownSuffix(test.relativePath);\n    if (testBase === sourceBase) {\n      return true;\n    }\n    if (dirname(test.relativePath) === sourceDir && testBase.startsWith(sourceBase)) {\n      return true;\n    }\n    if (dirname(test.relativePath).endsWith(`${sourceDir}/__tests__`) && testBase.includes(sourceBase)) {\n      return true;\n    }\n    return importedSpecifiers(test.sourceFile).some((specifier) => {\n      const resolved = resolveImportSpecifier(test.relativePath, specifier, sourcePaths, pathAliases);\n      return resolved === source.relativePath;\n    });\n  });\n}\n\nfunction weakSymbolTests(source: FileUnit, tests: readonly FileUnit[]): readonly FileUnit[] {\n  const symbolNames = exportedSymbols(source.sourceFile);\n  if (symbolNames.length === 0) {\n    return [];\n  }\n  return tests.filter((test) => symbolNames.some((symbolName) => symbolName.length > 2 && test.text.includes(symbolName)));\n}\n\nfunction basenameWithoutKnownSuffix(path: string): string {\n  return basename(path)\n    .replace(/\\.(spec|test)\\.(ts|tsx)$/, '')\n    .replace(/\\.(controller|resolver|handler|guard|strategy|middleware|interceptor|service|entity|module|component)\\.(ts|tsx)$/, '')\n    .replace(/\\.(ts|tsx)$/, '');\n}\n\nfunction importedSpecifiers(sourceFile: ts.SourceFile): string[] {\n  const imports: string[] = [];\n  for (const statement of sourceFile.statements) {\n    if (ts.isImportDeclaration(statement) && ts.isStringLiteral(statement.moduleSpecifier)) {\n      imports.push(statement.moduleSpecifier.text);\n    }\n  }\n  return imports;\n}\n\nfunction resolveImportSpecifier(\n  importingPath: string,\n  specifier: string,\n  sourcePaths: ReadonlySet<string>,\n  pathAliases: readonly PathAlias[],\n): string | undefined {\n  const candidates: string[] = [];\n  if (specifier.startsWith('.')) {\n    candidates.push(normalizePath(join(dirname(importingPath), specifier)));\n  } else {\n    for (const alias of pathAliases) {\n      if (!specifier.startsWith(alias.prefix) || !specifier.endsWith(alias.suffix)) {\n        continue;\n      }\n      const middle = specifier.slice(alias.prefix.length, specifier.length - alias.suffix.length);\n      for (const target of alias.targets) {\n        candidates.push(target.replace('*', middle));\n      }\n    }\n  }\n  for (const candidate of candidates.flatMap(candidatePathVariants)) {\n    if (sourcePaths.has(candidate)) {\n      return candidate;\n    }\n  }\n  return undefined;\n}\n\nfunction candidatePathVariants(candidate: string): string[] {\n  const normalized = normalizePath(candidate).replace(/^\\.\\//, '');\n  return [\n    normalized,\n    `${normalized}.ts`,\n    `${normalized}.tsx`,\n    `${normalized}/index.ts`,\n    `${normalized}/index.tsx`,\n  ];\n}\n\nfunction readPathAliases(workspaceRoot: string): readonly PathAlias[] {\n  const path = resolve(workspaceRoot, 'tsconfig.base.json');\n  if (!workspacePathExists(path)) {\n    return [];\n  }\n  try {\n    const parsed = JSON.parse(readWorkspaceFile(path)) as {\n      compilerOptions?: { paths?: Record<string, readonly string[]> };\n    };\n    return Object.entries(parsed.compilerOptions?.paths ?? {}).map(([key, targets]) => {\n      const starIndex = key.indexOf('*');\n      return {\n        prefix: starIndex >= 0 ? key.slice(0, starIndex) : key,\n        suffix: starIndex >= 0 ? key.slice(starIndex + 1) : '',\n        targets: targets.map((target) => normalizePath(target)),\n      };\n    });\n  } catch {\n    return [];\n  }\n}\n\nfunction exportedSymbols(sourceFile: ts.SourceFile): string[] {\n  const symbols = new Set<string>();\n  visit(sourceFile, (node) => {\n    if ((ts.isClassDeclaration(node) || ts.isFunctionDeclaration(node) || ts.isInterfaceDeclaration(node) || ts.isTypeAliasDeclaration(node)) && node.name) {\n      const modifiers = ts.canHaveModifiers(node) ? ts.getModifiers(node) ?? [] : [];\n      if (modifiers.some((modifier) => modifier.kind === ts.SyntaxKind.ExportKeyword)) {\n        symbols.add(node.name.text);\n      }\n    }\n  });\n  return Array.from(symbols).sort();\n}\n\nfunction readFileUnit(path: string, workspaceRoot: string): FileUnit {\n  const text = readWorkspaceFile(path);\n  return {\n    path,\n    relativePath: normalizePath(relative(workspaceRoot, path)),\n    text,\n    sourceFile: ts.createSourceFile(path, text, ts.ScriptTarget.Latest, true),\n    isTest: isTestFile(path),\n  };\n}\n\nfunction collectSourceAndTestFiles(root: string): readonly string[] {\n  return collectFiles(root, {\n    extensions: ['.ts', '.tsx'],\n    // E13 FP class (2): archived corpus (e.g. migrations/.archive/<timestamp>/)\n    // is dead code — it can neither need test coverage nor PROVIDE it (an\n    // archived spec must not satisfy a live source), so it is excluded from\n    // the scan entirely rather than special-cased at finding time.\n    includeFile: (name, path) => !name.endsWith('.d.ts') && !isArchivedWorkspacePath(path),\n  });\n}\n\nfunction isTestFile(path: string): boolean {\n  const normalized = normalizePath(path);\n  return /(__tests__|\\.spec\\.|\\.test\\.)/.test(normalized);\n}\n\nfunction resolveInsideWorkspace(workspaceRoot: string, requestedPath: string): string {\n  return resolveAdapterPath(workspaceRoot, requestedPath);\n}\n\nfunction visit(node: ts.Node, visitor: (node: ts.Node) => void): void {\n  visitor(node);\n  ts.forEachChild(node, (child) => visit(child, visitor));\n}\n\nfunction compareById(a: { readonly id: string }, b: { readonly id: string }): number {\n  return a.id.localeCompare(b.id);\n}\n\nfunction normalizePath(path: string): string {\n  return normalizeWorkspacePath(path);\n}\n\nfunction readStdin(): Promise<string> {\n  return new Promise((resolvePromise, reject) => {\n    let input = '';\n    process.stdin.setEncoding('utf8');\n    // setEncoding('utf8') makes every chunk a string at runtime, but the\n    // stream's declared chunk type stays `string | Buffer` — concatenating the\n    // union is what the type checker rejects. Narrow at the boundary rather\n    // than widening `input` (kernel-dead-wire-adapter is the converged shape).\n    process.stdin.on('data', (chunk: string | Buffer) => {\n      input += typeof chunk === 'string' ? chunk : chunk.toString('utf8');\n    });\n    process.stdin.on('end', () => resolvePromise(input));\n    process.stdin.on('error', reject);\n  });\n}\n\nasync function main(): Promise<void> {\n  const rawInput = await readStdin();\n  const input = rawInput.trim().length > 0 ? (JSON.parse(rawInput) as AdapterInput) : {};\n  process.stdout.write(`${JSON.stringify(analyzeTestGaps(input))}\\n`);\n}\n\nif (require.main === module) {\n  main().catch((error: unknown) => {\n    const message = error instanceof Error ? error.message : String(error);\n    process.stderr.write(`${message}\\n`);\n    process.exit(1);\n  });\n}\n","numLines":494,"startLine":1,"totalLines":494}}}

{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_017Ha1M9JrTz7yNeaY5PyRpy","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t/**\n2\t * Bootstrap From Scratch\n3\t * ============================================================================\n4\t *\n5\t * Proves that the fresh-volume init pipeline — `infrastructure/docker/init-scripts/*`\n6\t * + every service's TypeORM migration chain — produces a clean, complete\n7\t * platform schema when run against an empty Postgres.\n8\t *\n9\t * # Why this test exists\n10\t *\n11\t * The codebase's bootstrap pipeline silently broke when several\n12\t * `CREATE TABLE` migrations were squashed out of source. Production survived\n13\t * because nobody did a fresh-volume init for months — every existing\n14\t * environment had the squashed-out tables already on disk. The first\n15\t * fresh-volume deploy crashed at `1781100000000-ConvertTimestampToTimestamptz`\n16\t * trying to ALTER `auth.users.mfaLockedUntil` (a column whose CREATE was\n17\t * gone). That is the exact regression class this invariant guards against.\n18\t *\n19\t * # What it does\n20\t *\n21\t *   1. Spin up `timescale/timescaledb-ha:pg16` (the production image, by\n22\t *      digest pinned to docker-compose.infra.yml).\n23\t *   2. Copy `infrastructure/docker/init-scripts/` into\n24\t *      `/docker-entrypoint-initdb.d/` — Postgres runs every script in\n25\t *      lexical order on first boot of an empty PGDATA volume.\n26\t *   3. Connect, then for every service that owns a TypeORM migration\n27\t *      directory, dynamically load every migration class and run them\n28\t *      through a per-service DataSource bound to that service's schema.\n29\t *   4. Assert schema-level invariants: schemas exist, the previously\n30\t *      missing baseline tables now exist, the previously missing\n31\t *      `auth.users` columns now exist, the sensor hypertable was\n32\t *      created, db-migrate can owner-align a real tenant's continuous\n33\t *      aggregates, and each service's migration ledger is non-empty\n34\t *      (proving the migrations actually ran).\n35\t *\n36\t * # When this test fails\n37\t *\n38\t *   - A migration that creates a baseline table was squashed →\n39\t *     restore the CREATE in the earliest service migration. (This is\n40\t *     the original regression class.)\n41\t *   - An init-script SQL file was deleted without an equivalent\n42\t *     migration → either restore the script OR write a migration that\n43\t *     creates the same tables idempotently.\n44\t *   - A new entity was added without a corresponding CREATE TABLE\n45\t *     migration AND without an init-script entry → add the migration.\n46\t *   - The init scripts assume a privilege the bootstrap POSTGRES_USER\n47\t *     does not have on a fresh container → fix the script to use\n48\t *     a less-privileged path or supply additional bootstrap material.\n49\t *\n50\t * # Why testcontainers (not the shared TestDatabase pool)\n51\t *\n52\t * The other e2e/integration specs assert state of an *already-running*\n53\t * database that the dev/CI infra brought up — they're checking\n54\t * application-level behaviour. This test asserts the bootstrap ITSELF\n55\t * works on an EMPTY volume. It MUST start with no PGDATA, run the init\n56\t * scripts, then run the migration chain. testcontainers is the only\n57\t * way to get that hermetic guarantee inside a Jest worker.\n58\t *\n59\t * # Runtime\n60\t *\n61\t * ~3-5 minutes on a cold runner: image pull + init-script execution +\n62\t * 100+ migrations across 13 services. CI gate runs only on PRs that\n63\t * touch `apps/*\\/src/**\\/migrations/**` or\n64\t * `infrastructure/docker/init-scripts/**` (see CI workflow\n65\t * `.github/workflows/db-migration-check.yml` integration; if the gate\n66\t * is not yet wired, `npm run test:bootstrap` is the local entry point).\n67\t */\n68\t\n69\timport { readdirSync, statSync, existsSync } from 'fs';\n70\timport { createRequire } from 'module';\n71\timport { join, resolve } from 'path';\n72\t\n73\timport { Logger } from '@nestjs/common';\n74\timport { ConfigService } from '@nestjs/config';\n75\timport { GenericContainer, type StartedTestContainer, Wait } from 'testcontainers';\n76\timport { DataSource, type MigrationInterface } from 'typeorm';\n77\t\n78\t// ADR-031 Platform Bootstrap Atom — runs the schema/role/extension/function/\n79\t// shared-table DDL contract that init-scripts USED to own pre-cutover. The\n80\t// init-scripts directory copied below now contains only `01-init-databases.sql`\n81\t// (initdb-only DB GRANTs). Schemas, roles, functions, and shared.* tables\n82\t// are created by the call to runPlatformBootstrap() below before any\n83\t// per-service migration loop runs.\n84\timport {\n85\t  runPlatformBootstrap,\n86\t  resolvePlatformBootstrapSqlDir,\n87\t} from '../platform-bootstrap.service';\n88\timport { ensureTenantSensorContinuousAggregateAuthority } from '../tenant-sensor-continuous-aggregate-authority';\n89\t\n90\t// Constructor type for the @Entity / migration classes this spec loads\n91\t// dynamically. TypeORM's DataSource `entities`/`migrations` options accept\n92\t// `Function`, but `@typescript-eslint/no-unsafe-function-type` (correctly)\n93\t// rejects the bare `Function` type; a precise construct signature is both\n94\t// assignable to TypeORM's `Function` slot AND keeps the collected values\n95\t// typed as classes rather than arbitrary callables.\n96\ttype EntityClass = new (...args: never[]) => object;\n97\t\n98\t// Synchronous module loader bound to this spec's location. `import`/`await\n99\t// import()` cannot be used here: loadMigrationClassesFromDir is invoked inside\n100\t// `it.each(...)` arguments that Jest evaluates synchronously at collection\n101\t// time, so the load must be synchronous. `createRequire(__filename)` resolves\n102\t// migration classes and the cross-package schema-drift-validator through\n103\t// ts-jest at runtime, so the static type-check graph never reaches across\n104\t// project rootDirs — the same dynamic-load pattern the db-migrate\n105\t// platform-bootstrap.integration.spec.ts relies on.\n106\tconst requireModule = createRequire(__filename);\n107\t\n108\t// Structured logger for the (rare) entity-load skip path — `console.*` is\n109\t// banned platform-wide; the NestJS Logger keeps the diagnostic on the same\n110\t// structured-logging path the runtime services use.\n111\tconst logger = new Logger('bootstrap-from-scratch');\n112\t\n113\t// Local narrowing helper. db-migrate has @typescript-eslint/no-non-null-assertion\n114\t// ENABLED (unlike the e2e project, whose assertDefined SSoT this replaces), so a\n115\t// bare `!` is banned here. assertDefined narrows `T | undefined` to `T` and fails\n116\t// loudly at the assertion site instead of surfacing as a cryptic\n117\t// \"cannot read properties of undefined\" further downstream.\n118\tfunction assertDefined<T>(value: T | null | undefined, message?: string): T {\n119\t  if (value === null || value === undefined) {\n120\t    throw new Error(message ?? 'assertDefined: expected a value but received null/undefined');\n121\t  }\n122\t  return value;\n123\t}\n124\t\n125\t// Dynamic load of the backend-common schema-drift-validator. Importing\n126\t// schema-drift-validator.service.ts directly via `import { ... }` would pull a\n127\t// file from another Nx project into db-migrate's type-check graph. ts-jest\n128\t// resolves this lazily at runtime (same pattern the migration loader uses for\n129\t// migration classes), so the validator's stable runtime contract is exercised\n130\t// without a static cross-project import. From this spec's location\n131\t// (apps/db-migrate/src/__tests__/) the repo root is four levels up.\n132\ttype SchemaDriftValidatorFactory = (\n133\t  serviceName: string,\n134\t) => new (ds: DataSource, cs: ConfigService) => { onApplicationBootstrap(): Promise<void> };\n135\tfunction loadDriftValidatorFactory(): SchemaDriftValidatorFactory {\n136\t  const mod = requireModule(\n137\t    '../../../../libs/backend-common/src/database/schema-drift-validator.service',\n138\t  ) as {\n139\t    createSchemaDriftValidator: SchemaDriftValidatorFactory;\n140\t  };\n141\t  return mod.createSchemaDriftValidator;\n142\t}\n143\t\n144\t// The same image+digest the production stack uses — see\n145\t// docker-compose.infra.yml line 12. Pinning by digest guarantees the\n146\t// init-script behaviour we're proving here matches what runs in\n147\t// production. Bumping the digest is a deliberate review surface.\n148\tconst POSTGRES_IMAGE =\n149\t  'timescale/timescaledb-ha:pg16@sha256:b3d038d0a0757df8a5ec0a94ba68d9ad57b0e16100a024cf4b370c77ad5645f7';\n150\t\n151\tconst DATABASE_NAME = 'aquaculture';\n152\tconst DATABASE_USER = 'aquaculture';\n153\tconst DATABASE_PASSWORD = 'aquaculture-test';\n154\tconst DB_MIGRATE_DDL_AUTHORITY_ENV = 'DB_MIGRATE_DDL_AUTHORITY';\n155\tconst SERVICE_ROLE_PASSWORD_ENVS = [\n156\t  'AUTH_SERVICE_DB_PASS',\n157\t  'FARM_SERVICE_DB_PASS',\n158\t  'SENSOR_SERVICE_DB_PASS',\n159\t  'BILLING_SERVICE_DB_PASS',\n160\t  'HR_SERVICE_DB_PASS',\n161\t  'ALERT_SERVICE_DB_PASS',\n162\t  'ADMIN_SERVICE_DB_PASS',\n163\t  'GATEWAY_SERVICE_DB_PASS',\n164\t  'NOTIFICATION_SERVICE_DB_PASS',\n165\t  'HYDROPONICS_SERVICE_DB_PASS',\n166\t  'AI_SERVICE_DB_PASS',\n167\t  'MESSAGING_SERVICE_DB_PASS',\n168\t  'OBSERVABILITY_SERVICE_DB_PASS',\n169\t  'EVENT_STORE_SERVICE_DB_PASS',\n170\t  'CONFIG_SERVICE_DB_PASS',\n171\t] as const;\n172\t\n173\t// Repo root, derived from this file's location:\n174\t//   apps/db-migrate/src/__tests__/bootstrap-from-scratch.spec.ts -> ../../../..\n175\t// (mirrors how the sibling platform-bootstrap.integration.spec.ts resolves it).\n176\tconst REPO_ROOT = resolve(__dirname, '..', '..', '..', '..');\n177\tconst INIT_SCRIPTS_DIR = join(REPO_ROOT, 'infrastructure', 'docker', 'init-scripts');\n178\t\n179\tasync function runWithDbMigrateDdlAuthority<T>(operation: () => Promise<T>): Promise<T> {\n180\t  const previous = process.env[DB_MIGRATE_DDL_AUTHORITY_ENV];\n181\t  process.env[DB_MIGRATE_DDL_AUTHORITY_ENV] = '1';\n182\t  try {\n183\t    return await operation();\n184\t  } finally {\n185\t    if (previous === undefined) {\n186\t      Reflect.deleteProperty(process.env, DB_MIGRATE_DDL_AUTHORITY_ENV);\n187\t    } else {\n188\t      process.env[DB_MIGRATE_DDL_AUTHORITY_ENV] = previous;\n189\t    }\n190\t  }\n191\t}\n192\t\n193\t/**\n194\t * Per-service migration manifest.\n195\t *\n196\t * Each entry maps a service to:\n197\t *   - `schema`: the Postgres schema this service owns. The DataSource is\n198\t *     bound to it so the migration ledger lands in `<schema>.migrations`.\n199\t *   - `migrationsDir`: the on-disk path containing the service's\n200\t *     timestamped TypeORM migration files. Two patterns exist in this\n201\t *     codebase (historical drift, ADR-011 §\"Migration Runners\"):\n202\t *       * `apps/<svc>/src/migrations/`         (auth, admin-api, messaging, event-store)\n203\t *       * `apps/<svc>/src/database/migrations/` (everything else)\n204\t *     This manifest reflects the actual on-disk layout — keep in sync\n205\t *     when adding a service.\n206\t *\n207\t * Order matters: services with cross-schema FK migrations (e.g.\n208\t * messaging.tenant_ai_settings -> auth.tenants) must run after the\n209\t * schema they reference. We run auth FIRST for that reason; everything\n210\t * else is alphabetical.\n211\t */\n212\tinterface ServiceManifest {\n213\t  name: string;\n214\t  schema: string;\n215\t  migrationsDir: string;\n216\t  /**\n217\t   * `entitiesGlob`: optional glob (relative to repo root) that matches\n218\t   * every `*.entity.ts` file owned by this service. When set, the\n219\t   * entity-surface-vs-DB matrix (assertEntitySurfaceMatchesDb) iterates\n220\t   * the files under this glob and asserts each entity's table surface\n221\t   * exists in the DB. Services without entities (gateway-api) leave it\n222\t   * unset.\n223\t   */\n224\t  entitiesGlob?: string;\n225\t}\n226\t\n227\tconst SERVICES: ServiceManifest[] = [\n228\t  // auth FIRST — every other service's FKs reference auth.tenants.\n229\t  {\n230\t    name: 'auth-service',\n231\t    schema: 'auth',\n232\t    migrationsDir: 'apps/auth-service/src/migrations',\n233\t    entitiesGlob: 'apps/auth-service/src',\n234\t  },\n235\t  // Then the rest, ordered to minimize cross-schema FK ordering hazards.\n236\t  {\n237\t    name: 'farm-service',\n238\t    schema: 'farm',\n239\t    migrationsDir: 'apps/farm-service/src/database/migrations',\n240\t    entitiesGlob: 'apps/farm-service/src',\n241\t  },\n242\t  {\n243\t    name: 'sensor-service',\n244\t    schema: 'sensor',\n245\t    migrationsDir: 'apps/sensor-service/src/database/migrations',\n246\t    entitiesGlob: 'apps/sensor-service/src',\n247\t  },\n248\t  {\n249\t    name: 'hr-service',\n250\t    schema: 'hr',\n251\t    migrationsDir: 'apps/hr-service/src/database/migrations',\n252\t    entitiesGlob: 'apps/hr-service/src',\n253\t  },\n254\t  {\n255\t    name: 'messaging-service',\n256\t    schema: 'messaging',\n257\t    migrationsDir: 'apps/messaging-service/src/migrations',\n258\t    entitiesGlob: 'apps/messaging-service/src',\n259\t  },\n260\t  {\n261\t    name: 'alert-engine',\n262\t    schema: 'alert',\n263\t    migrationsDir: 'apps/alert-engine/src/database/migrations',\n264\t    entitiesGlob: 'apps/alert-engine/src',\n265\t  },\n266\t  {\n267\t    name: 'billing-service',\n268\t    schema: 'billing',\n269\t    migrationsDir: 'apps/billing-service/src/database/migrations',\n270\t    entitiesGlob: 'apps/billing-service/src',\n271\t  },\n272\t  {\n273\t    name: 'notification-service',\n274\t    schema: 'notification',\n275\t    migrationsDir: 'apps/notification-service/src/database/migrations',\n276\t    entitiesGlob: 'apps/notification-service/src',\n277\t  },\n278\t  {\n279\t    name: 'observability-service',\n280\t    schema: 'observability',\n281\t    migrationsDir: 'apps/observability-service/src/database/migrations',\n282\t    entitiesGlob: 'apps/observability-service/src',\n283\t  },\n284\t  {\n285\t    name: 'admin-api-service',\n286\t    schema: 'admin',\n287\t    migrationsDir: 'apps/admin-api-service/src/migrations',\n288\t    entitiesGlob: 'apps/admin-api-service/src',\n289\t  },\n290\t  {\n291\t    name: 'event-store-service',\n292\t    schema: 'event_store',\n293\t    migrationsDir: 'apps/event-store-service/src/migrations',\n294\t    entitiesGlob: 'apps/event-store-service/src',\n295\t  },\n296\t  // Wave 4-A.2 Dalga 5 — close the manifest gap. The 4 services\n297\t  // below were absent from the original 11-service manifest, so the\n298\t  // bootstrap test passed even when their migration chains were empty\n299\t  // or their entity-surface drifted from the on-disk schema. Adding\n300\t  // them here closes the regression class:\n301\t  //\n302\t  //   hydroponics-service / ai-service — schema-per-tenant services\n303\t  //     with their own baseline migrations + entity surface.\n304\t  //   config-service — platform-level service. Migrations dir is\n305\t  //     intentionally empty (.gitkeep only) — the entity-surface\n306\t  //     check still runs, the migration runner is a no-op.\n307\t  //   gateway-api — pure HTTP fronting, owns NO entities and NO\n308\t  //     migrations. Listed in the manifest so the schema-ownership\n309\t  //     audit (Part B B.4) can assert the `gateway` schema exists\n310\t  //     and is owned by `gateway_service`. `entitiesGlob` is unset\n311\t  //     → entity-surface check skips it (correct: nothing to check).\n312\t  {\n313\t    name: 'hydroponics-service',\n314\t    schema: 'hydroponics',\n315\t    migrationsDir: 'apps/hydroponics-service/src/database/migrations',\n316\t    entitiesGlob: 'apps/hydroponics-service/src',\n317\t  },\n318\t  {\n319\t    name: 'ai-service',\n320\t    schema: 'ai',\n321\t    migrationsDir: 'apps/ai-service/src/database/migrations',\n322\t    entitiesGlob: 'apps/ai-service/src',\n323\t  },\n324\t  {\n325\t    name: 'config-service',\n326\t    schema: 'config',\n327\t    migrationsDir: 'apps/config-service/src/database/migrations',\n328\t    entitiesGlob: 'apps/config-service/src',\n329\t  },\n330\t  {\n331\t    name: 'gateway-api',\n332\t    schema: 'gateway',\n333\t    migrationsDir: 'apps/gateway-api/src/database/migrations',\n334\t    // gateway-api owns no entities — `entitiesGlob` intentionally unset.\n335\t  },\n336\t];\n337\t\n338\t/**\n339\t * Tables expected to be registered as TimescaleDB hypertables in the SOURCE\n340\t * schemas after the full bootstrap pipeline runs.\n341\t *\n342\t * `sensor_readings` is the retired per-reading store: nothing reads or writes\n343\t * it any more (SENSOR-HIGH-085 replaced it with an as-of projection over\n344\t * sensor_metrics), but Baseline still creates it and it still holds the\n345\t * historical rows, so its hypertable registration remains part of the bootstrap\n346\t * contract until F-085-DROP removes the table outright.\n347\t *\n348\t * `sensor_metrics` is the per-tenant channel-keyed telemetry hypertable. This\n349\t * list only covers the `sensor` source copy — the per-tenant copies come from\n350\t * replaying 1815000000000-CreateTenantSensorMetricsHypertable into each tenant\n351\t * schema, which this spec does not exercise because it never provisions a\n352\t * tenant. That gap is not cosmetic: DATA-CRITICAL-010 shows a fresh tenant\n353\t * provision aborts before reaching 1815 at all.\n354\t */\n355\tconst EXPECTED_HYPERTABLES: Array<{ schema: string; table: string }> = [\n356\t  { schema: 'sensor', table: 'sensor_readings' },\n357\t  { schema: 'sensor', table: 'sensor_metrics' },\n358\t];\n359\t\n360\t/**\n361\t * Schemas whose owning services run in schema-per-tenant mode (ADR-011 +\n362\t * libs/backend-common/.../tenant-aware-schemas.ts SSoT). Every such\n363\t * source schema MUST have RLS policies registered against its tables —\n364\t * otherwise the tenant_<uuid> clones inherit ZERO policies via\n365\t * CREATE TABLE LIKE INCLUDING ALL and a row-leakage hole opens.\n366\t *\n367\t * Asserted by Part A's RLS-policy probe (assertEntitySurfaceMatchesDb)\n368\t * and Part B B.5's TENANT_SCOPED audit. Both consumers import this\n369\t * constant via the SERVICES manifest.\n370\t */\n371\tconst RLS_REQUIRED_SCHEMAS: ReadonlySet<string> = new Set([\n372\t  'farm',\n373\t  'sensor',\n374\t  'hr',\n375\t  'messaging',\n376\t  'hydroponics',\n377\t  'ai',\n378\t  'alert',\n379\t]);\n380\t\n381\t/**\n382\t * Schemas that MUST exist after the init scripts have run, BEFORE any\n383\t * TypeORM migration touches the DB. These are created exclusively by\n384\t * `00-init-schemas.sh` in the init-scripts bind mount.\n385\t */\n386\tconst REQUIRED_SCHEMAS = [\n387\t  'auth',\n388\t  'farm',\n389\t  'sensor',\n390\t  'hr',\n391\t  'messaging',\n392\t  'hydroponics',\n393\t  'alert',\n394\t  'billing',\n395\t  'notification',\n396\t  'ai',\n397\t  'admin',\n398\t  'observability',\n399\t  'event_store',\n400\t  'shared',\n401\t  'gateway',\n402\t];\n403\t\n404\t/**\n405\t * Critical columns on `auth.users` that the previous regression class\n406\t * silently dropped. Each one was added by `1700000000000-CreateInitialSchema`\n407\t * after the squash damage was identified — proving they exist after a\n408\t * fresh-volume init is the exact failure mode this test guards.\n409\t */\n410\tconst CRITICAL_AUTH_USERS_COLUMNS = [\n411\t  'mfaLockedUntil',\n412\t  'mfaRecoveryCodes',\n413\t  'mfaFailedAttempts',\n414\t  'accessType',\n415\t  'notificationPreferences',\n416\t];\n417\t\n418\t/**\n419\t * Baseline tables in `auth` schema that were lost in the squash and\n420\t * restored by `1700000000000-CreateInitialSchema`. Asserting all 12 exist\n421\t * is the most direct proof the migration chain rebuilt the auth surface.\n422\t */\n423\tconst AUTH_BASELINE_TABLES = [\n424\t  'refresh_tokens',\n425\t  'webauthn_credentials',\n426\t  'user_module_assignments',\n427\t  'mobile_user_settings',\n428\t  'modules',\n429\t  'announcements',\n430\t  'announcement_acknowledgments',\n431\t  'message_threads',\n432\t  'messages',\n433\t  'support_tickets',\n434\t  'ticket_comments',\n435\t  'audit_logs',\n436\t];\n437\t\n438\t/**\n439\t * Dynamically load every migration class from a directory.\n440\t *\n441\t * Strategy: iterate the directory, filter to `<13-digit>-<Name>.ts` files\n442\t * (the codebase's enforced filename convention — see\n443\t * .github/workflows/db-migration-check.yml step \"Validate migration\n444\t * filename...\"), require each, and pull the default-or-named export\n445\t * whose name ends with the timestamp. Migration classes follow the\n446\t * convention `<Name><timestamp>` (e.g. `CreateInitialSchema1700000000000`).\n447\t */\n448\tfunction loadMigrationClassesFromDir(absDir: string): Array<new () => MigrationInterface> {\n449\t  // Wave 4-A.2 Dalga 5 — services that own a directory but ship no\n450\t  // migrations yet (config-service: .gitkeep only; gateway-api:\n451\t  // directory absent entirely) are valid manifest entries; return an\n452\t  // empty class list so the per-service runMigrations() call is a no-op\n453\t  // rather than a hard failure.\n454\t  if (!existsSync(absDir)) {\n455\t    return [];\n456\t  }\n457\t\n458\t  let entries: string[];\n459\t  try {\n460\t    entries = readdirSync(absDir);\n461\t  } catch (err) {\n462\t    throw new Error(\n463\t      `Migration directory ${absDir} could not be read: ${(err as Error).message}. ` +\n464\t        `Update SERVICES manifest if the path moved.`,\n465\t    );\n466\t  }\n467\t\n468\t  const migrationFiles = entries\n469\t    .filter((f) => /^[0-9]{13}-[A-Z][A-Za-z0-9]*\\.ts$/.test(f))\n470\t    .filter((f) => !f.endsWith('.spec.ts') && !f.endsWith('.test.ts'))\n471\t    .sort(); // lexical sort = chronological because timestamps are zero-padded.\n472\t\n473\t  const classes: Array<new () => MigrationInterface> = [];\n474\t  for (const file of migrationFiles) {\n475\t    const fullPath = join(absDir, file);\n476\t    if (!statSync(fullPath).isFile()) continue;\n477\t\n478\t    // ts-jest transforms .ts files when loaded through requireModule here\n479\t    // because this spec runs under the ts-jest transform configured in\n480\t    // e2e/jest.config.ts. Each migration's `export class <Name>` becomes\n481\t    // a property on the loaded module object.\n482\t    const mod = requireModule(fullPath) as Record<string, unknown>;\n483\t    const candidate = Object.values(mod).find(\n484\t      (v): v is new () => MigrationInterface =>\n485\t        typeof v === 'function' && /^[A-Z]/.test(v.name) && /\\d{13}$/.test(v.name),\n486\t    );\n487\t    if (!candidate) {\n488\t      throw new Error(\n489\t        `Migration file ${fullPath} did not export a class whose name matches ` +\n490\t          `<PascalCaseName><13-digit-timestamp>. Filename + class-name convention ` +\n491\t          `is enforced by .github/workflows/db-migration-check.yml.`,\n492\t      );\n493\t    }\n494\t    classes.push(candidate);\n495\t  }\n496\t  return classes;\n497\t}\n498\t\n499\t/**\n500\t * Discover every `*.entity.ts` file under a service's source root.\n501\t *\n502\t * Used by the entity-surface coverage matrix (assertEntitySurfaceMatchesDb).\n503\t * Recurses through subdirectories because services scatter entities across\n504\t * `src/<feature>/entities/`, `src/<feature>/`, and the legacy\n505\t * `src/setup/entities/` location.\n506\t */\n507\tfunction findEntityFiles(absRoot: string): string[] {\n508\t  const out: string[] = [];\n509\t  if (!existsSync(absRoot)) return out;\n510\t  const stack: string[] = [absRoot];\n511\t  while (stack.length > 0) {\n512\t    // The `while (stack.length > 0)` guard makes `pop()` defined here;\n513\t    // assertDefined narrows it without a banned non-null assertion.\n514\t    const current = assertDefined(stack.pop());\n515\t    let entries: string[];\n516\t    try {\n517\t      entries = readdirSync(current);\n518\t    } catch {\n519\t      continue;\n520\t    }\n521\t    for (const name of entries) {\n522\t      // Skip migration directories — files there are CREATE TABLE\n523\t      // statements, not entity declarations. Skip __tests__ to avoid\n524\t      // pulling spec doubles into the metadata graph.\n525\t      if (name === 'migrations' || name === '__tests__' || name === 'node_modules') {\n526\t        continue;\n527\t      }\n528\t      const full = join(current, name);\n529\t      let st;\n530\t      try {\n531\t        st = statSync(full);\n532\t      } catch {\n533\t        continue;\n534\t      }\n535\t      if (st.isDirectory()) {\n536\t        stack.push(full);\n537\t        continue;\n538\t      }\n539\t      if (st.isFile() && /\\.entity\\.ts$/.test(name)) {\n540\t        out.push(full);\n541\t      }\n542\t    }\n543\t  }\n544\t  return out;\n545\t}\n546\t\n547\t/**\n548\t * Load every `@Entity` class from a service's source root.\n549\t *\n550\t * Strategy: ts-jest transforms .ts files on load, so each entity file\n551\t * registers itself with TypeORM's getMetadataArgsStorage() side-effect.\n552\t * After loading all files we instantiate a metadata-only DataSource\n553\t * to materialize EntityMetadata graphs that the surface-matrix probes\n554\t * iterate over.\n555\t */\n556\tfunction loadEntityClasses(absRoot: string): EntityClass[] {\n557\t  const files = findEntityFiles(absRoot);\n558\t  const classes: EntityClass[] = [];\n559\t  const loadFailures: string[] = [];\n560\t  for (const file of files) {\n561\t    let mod: Record<string, unknown>;\n562\t    try {\n563\t      mod = requireModule(file) as Record<string, unknown>;\n564\t    } catch (err) {\n565\t      // A .entity.ts that fails to load is NEVER safe to skip. Because the\n566\t      // require throws, the module body never executes — so its @Entity\n567\t      // decorators do NOT register with getMetadataArgsStorage and the entity\n568\t      // is fully ABSENT from the metadata graph. Silently continuing would let\n569\t      // that entity's table pass the schema-drift matrix by omission: a\n570\t      // genuinely missing table/column would go undetected. Collect every\n571\t      // failure and fail the suite loudly (see throw below).\n572\t      const message = `${file}: ${(err as Error).message}`;\n573\t      loadFailures.push(message);\n574\t      logger.error(`could not load entity file ${message}`);\n575\t      continue;\n576\t    }\n577\t    for (const v of Object.values(mod)) {\n578\t      if (isEntityClass(v)) {\n579\t        classes.push(v);\n580\t      }\n581\t    }\n582\t  }\n583\t  if (loadFailures.length > 0) {\n584\t    throw new Error(\n585\t      `loadEntityClasses(${absRoot}) failed to load ${loadFailures.length} ` +\n586\t        `entity file(s). An un-loadable entity silently drops out of the ` +\n587\t        `schema-drift matrix, so this is a hard failure — fix the ` +\n588\t        `compile/load error(s):\\n${loadFailures.map((f) => `  - ${f}`).join('\\n')}`,\n589\t    );\n590\t  }\n591\t  return classes;\n592\t}\n593\t\n594\t/** Narrow an unknown module export to a PascalCase-named class constructor. */\n595\tfunction isEntityClass(value: unknown): value is EntityClass {\n596\t  return typeof value === 'function' && /^[A-Z]/.test(value.name);\n597\t}\n598\t\n599\tinterface SurfaceProbeResult {\n600\t  schema: string;\n601\t  table: string;\n602\t  drift: string[];\n603\t}\n604\t\n605\t/**\n606\t * Assert that every entity registered for a service has a matching\n607\t * physical table, columns, FKs, indexes, and (for tenant-scoped\n608\t * schemas) at least one tenant_<uuid> RLS-policy clone.\n609\t *\n610\t * This is the generic helper the mission spec calls\n611\t * `assertEntitySurfaceMatchesDb`. Returns the per-entity drift list;\n612\t * the caller decides how to surface failures (one Jest case per drift\n613\t * gives the highest-signal CI output).\n614\t */\n615\tinterface PgConnInfo {\n616\t  host: string;\n617\t  port: number;\n618\t  username: string;\n619\t  password: string;\n620\t  database: string;\n621\t}\n622\t\n623\tasync function assertEntitySurfaceMatchesDb(\n624\t  serviceName: string,\n625\t  entityClasses: ReadonlyArray<EntityClass>,\n626\t  conn: PgConnInfo,\n627\t): Promise<SurfaceProbeResult[]> {\n628\t  // Build a metadata-only DataSource so EntityMetadata graphs are\n629\t  // materialized for FK + index introspection. We cannot use the\n630\t  // service's migration DataSource because that one is configured\n631\t  // with `migrations: [...]` and no entities. A short-lived metadata\n632\t  // DataSource avoids re-running migrations.\n633\t  const metadataDs = new DataSource({\n634\t    type: 'postgres',\n635\t    host: conn.host,\n636\t    port: conn.port,\n637\t    username: conn.username,\n638\t    password: conn.password,\n639\t    database: conn.database,\n640\t    entities: [...entityClasses],\n641\t    synchronize: false,\n642\t    migrationsRun: false,\n643\t  });\n644\t  await metadataDs.initialize();\n645\t\n646\t  const results: SurfaceProbeResult[] = [];\n647\t  try {\n648\t    for (const meta of metadataDs.entityMetadatas) {\n649\t      // Resolve declared schema. Tenant-aware entities legitimately\n650\t      // omit `schema:` (the SchemaManagerService routes them at\n651\t      // provision time). For source-table existence we look in the\n652\t      // canonical service schema derived from the manifest.\n653\t      const declaredSchema =\n654\t        meta.schema ??\n655\t        // Fall back to the service's canonical schema — tenant-aware\n656\t        // entities use this path.\n657\t        SERVICES.find((s) => s.name === serviceName)?.schema ??\n658\t        'public';\n659\t\n660\t      const drift: string[] = [];\n661\t      const tableName = meta.tableName;\n662\t\n663\t      // (a) Table exists in declared OR routed schema. We accept the\n664\t      //     declared schema as primary; tenant_* schemas are a\n665\t      //     secondary residence and skipped here (Part C covers them).\n666\t      const tableRows: Array<{ schemaname: string }> = await metadataDs.query(\n667\t        `SELECT schemaname FROM pg_tables\n668\t         WHERE tablename = $1 AND schemaname = $2`,\n669\t        [tableName, declaredSchema],\n670\t      );\n671\t      if (tableRows.length === 0) {\n672\t        drift.push(`table missing: ${declaredSchema}.${tableName} not found in pg_tables`);\n673\t        results.push({ schema: declaredSchema, table: tableName, drift });\n674\t        continue;\n675\t      }\n676\t\n677\t      // (b) Column existence + data-type compatibility per @Column.\n678\t      const columnRows: Array<{ column_name: string; data_type: string }> = await metadataDs.query(\n679\t        `SELECT column_name, data_type FROM information_schema.columns\n680\t           WHERE table_schema = $1 AND table_name = $2`,\n681\t        [declaredSchema, tableName],\n682\t      );\n683\t      const dbColumns = new Map(columnRows.map((r) => [r.column_name, r.data_type]));\n684\t      for (const col of meta.columns) {\n685\t        if (!dbColumns.has(col.databaseName)) {\n686\t          drift.push(\n687\t            `column missing: ${declaredSchema}.${tableName}.${col.databaseName} ` +\n688\t              `(entity declares but DB has no such column)`,\n689\t          );\n690\t        }\n691\t      }\n692\t\n693\t      // (c) FKs: every @ManyToOne relation has a pg_constraint row.\n694\t      //     We count FKs whose constrained relation matches the table;\n695\t      //     mismatched count is the drift signal (TypeORM emits one\n696\t      //     constraint per relation).\n697\t      const expectedFkCount = meta.relations.filter((r) => r.relationType === 'many-to-one').length;\n698\t      if (expectedFkCount > 0) {\n699\t        const fkRows: Array<{ count: string }> = await metadataDs.query(\n700\t          `SELECT COUNT(*)::text AS count FROM pg_constraint c\n701\t             JOIN pg_class t ON t.oid = c.conrelid\n702\t             JOIN pg_namespace n ON n.oid = t.relnamespace\n703\t           WHERE c.contype = 'f'\n704\t             AND n.nspname = $1\n705\t             AND t.relname = $2`,\n706\t          [declaredSchema, tableName],\n707\t        );\n708\t        const actual = parseInt(fkRows[0]?.count ?? '0', 10);\n709\t        if (actual < expectedFkCount) {\n710\t          drift.push(\n711\t            `fk drift: ${declaredSchema}.${tableName} declares ${expectedFkCount} ` +\n712\t              `@ManyToOne relation(s) but pg_constraint has only ${actual} FK(s)`,\n713\t          );\n714\t        }\n715\t      }\n716\t\n717\t      // (d) Indexes: every @Index has a pg_indexes row. We do a\n718\t      //     count-only check because TypeORM-generated index names\n719\t      //     are unstable across versions; the drift signal is \"DB\n720\t      //     has fewer indexes than the entity declares\" — extra\n721\t      //     indexes are fine.\n722\t      const expectedIxCount = meta.indices.length;\n723\t      if (expectedIxCount > 0) {\n724\t        const ixRows: Array<{ count: string }> = await metadataDs.query(\n725\t          `SELECT COUNT(*)::text AS count FROM pg_indexes\n726\t           WHERE schemaname = $1 AND tablename = $2`,\n727\t          [declaredSchema, tableName],\n728\t        );\n729\t        const actual = parseInt(ixRows[0]?.count ?? '0', 10);\n730\t        // The PK always has its own index — entity @Index list does\n731\t        // not include it. Accept actual >= expectedIxCount + 0 (PK\n732\t        // index counted, declared @Index entries each add one).\n733\t        if (actual < expectedIxCount) {\n734\t          drift.push(\n735\t            `index drift: ${declaredSchema}.${tableName} declares ${expectedIxCount} ` +\n736\t              `@Index entries, pg_indexes has only ${actual} (excluding PK)`,\n737\t          );\n738\t        }\n739\t      }\n740\t\n741\t      // (e) For tenant-scoped schemas: at least one tenant_<uuid> clone\n742\t      //     must carry an RLS policy on the cloned table. The bootstrap\n743\t      //     test runs against an empty-tenant DB (no tenants\n744\t      //     provisioned), so we cannot fail on \"no clone exists\" — we\n745\t      //     only fail when a clone EXISTS and is missing RLS. Part C\n746\t      //     spec covers the active-clone shape with a real tenant.\n747\t      if (RLS_REQUIRED_SCHEMAS.has(declaredSchema)) {\n748\t        const cloneRows: Array<{\n749\t          tablename: string;\n750\t          schemaname: string;\n751\t        }> = await metadataDs.query(\n752\t          `SELECT tablename, schemaname FROM pg_tables\n753\t           WHERE schemaname ~ '^tenant_[a-f0-9]{16}$'\n754\t             AND tablename = $1`,\n755\t          [tableName],\n756\t        );\n757\t        for (const cr of cloneRows) {\n758\t          const policyRows: Array<{ count: string }> = await metadataDs.query(\n759\t            `SELECT COUNT(*)::text AS count FROM pg_policies\n760\t             WHERE schemaname = $1 AND tablename = $2`,\n761\t            [cr.schemaname, cr.tablename],\n762\t          );\n763\t          if (parseInt(policyRows[0]?.count ??
