view_schema = $1\n          ORDER BY view_name`,\n        [tenantSchema],\n      )) as Array<{ view_name: string; view_owner: string; runtime_can_select: boolean }>;\n\n      expect(aggregates).toEqual([\n        {\n          view_name: 'metrics_1day',\n          view_owner: 'sensor_aggregate_owner',\n          runtime_can_select: true,\n        },\n        {\n          view_name: 'metrics_1hour',\n          view_owner: 'sensor_aggregate_owner',\n          runtime_can_select: true,\n        },\n        {\n          view_name: 'metrics_1min',\n          view_owner: 'sensor_aggregate_owner',\n          runtime_can_select: true,\n        },\n      ]);\n    } finally {\n      try {\n        await queryRunner.query(`DROP SCHEMA IF EXISTS \"${tenantSchema}\" CASCADE`);\n      } finally {\n        await queryRunner.release();\n      }\n    }\n  });\n\n  it('billing.plans exists (squash-restoration target)', async () => {\n    const rows = await probeDs().query<{ table_name: string }[]>(\n      `SELECT table_name FROM information_schema.tables\n       WHERE table_schema = 'billing' AND table_name = 'plans'`,\n    );\n    expect(rows.length).toBe(1);\n  });\n\n  it('billing.payments exists (squash-restoration target)', async () => {\n    const rows = await probeDs().query<{ table_name: string }[]>(\n      `SELECT table_name FROM information_schema.tables\n       WHERE table_schema = 'billing' AND table_name = 'payments'`,\n    );\n    expect(rows.length).toBe(1);\n  });\n\n  it('alert.alert_rules exists (alert-engine baseline)', async () => {\n    const rows = await probeDs().query<{ table_name: string }[]>(\n      `SELECT table_name FROM information_schema.tables\n       WHERE table_schema = 'alert' AND table_name = 'alert_rules'`,\n    );\n    expect(rows.length).toBe(1);\n  });\n\n  it('admin.audit_logs exists (created by 11-service-audit-tables.sql)', async () => {\n    const rows = await probeDs().query<{ table_name: string }[]>(\n      `SELECT table_name FROM information_schema.tables\n       WHERE table_schema = 'admin' AND table_name = 'audit_logs'`,\n    );\n    expect(rows.length).toBe(1);\n  });\n\n  it('shared schema contains the 4 cross-service tables (audit_logs, gdpr_data_requests, user_consents, access_logs)', async () => {\n    // user_permissions retired 2026-07-12 (ADR-042, ORPHAN-HIGH-378):\n    // the canonical shared set is audit_logs, gdpr_data_requests,\n    // user_consents, access_logs.\n    const rows = await probeDs().query<{ table_name: string }[]>(\n      `SELECT table_name FROM information_schema.tables\n       WHERE table_schema = 'shared' AND table_name = ANY($1::text[])`,\n      [['audit_logs', 'gdpr_data_requests', 'user_consents', 'access_logs']],\n    );\n    const found = new Set(rows.map((r) => r.table_name));\n    for (const t of ['audit_logs', 'gdpr_data_requests', 'user_consents', 'access_logs']) {\n      if (!found.has(t)) {\n        throw new Error(\n          `shared.${t} missing after platform bootstrap. Check ` +\n            `apps/db-migrate/src/sql/platform-bootstrap/006-shared-schema-tables.sql.`,\n        );\n      }\n    }\n  });\n\n  // ---------------------------------------------------------------------\n  // Part A.3 — Migration ledger completeness.\n  //\n  // Failure mode this catches: TypeORM \"thought it ran\" but skipped\n  // migrations. Empirically observed when the migrations array passed\n  // to TypeOrmModule.forRoot was a stale glob that resolved to fewer\n  // files than the migrations directory holds, and runMigrations()\n  // returned without error because every entry it knew about was\n  // already applied. The ledger row count then drifts below the\n  // file-system count and silent under-application goes undetected\n  // until a fresh deploy reveals missing tables.\n  //\n  // Assertion: count(<schema>.migrations rows) == count(migration files\n  // on disk that match the timestamp+PascalCase regex). Skip services\n  // with empty migration sets (gateway-api, config-service: those have\n  // NO ledger because we skipped runMigrations() entirely).\n  // ---------------------------------------------------------------------\n  it.each(\n    SERVICES.filter(\n      (s) => loadMigrationClassesFromDir(join(REPO_ROOT, s.migrationsDir)).length > 0,\n    ),\n  )(\n    'service \"$name\" migration ledger row count matches on-disk migration file count',\n    async ({ name, schema, migrationsDir }) => {\n      const ds = dataSources.get(name);\n      if (!ds || !ds.isInitialized) {\n        throw new Error(`${name} DataSource not initialized.`);\n      }\n      const onDiskCount = loadMigrationClassesFromDir(join(REPO_ROOT, migrationsDir)).length;\n      const rows = await ds.query<{ count: string }[]>(\n        `SELECT COUNT(*)::text AS count FROM \"${schema}\".migrations`,\n      );\n      const ledgerCount = parseInt(rows[0]?.count ?? '0', 10);\n      if (ledgerCount !== onDiskCount) {\n        throw new Error(\n          `${schema}.migrations ledger row count drift: on-disk migrations dir has ` +\n            `${onDiskCount} timestamped files, ledger has ${ledgerCount} rows. ` +\n            `Either runMigrations() silently skipped some files (TypeORM cache or ` +\n            `metadata-loader bug) or a file landed on disk without being included ` +\n            `in the per-service migrations[] manifest.`,\n        );\n      }\n    },\n  );\n\n  // ---------------------------------------------------------------------\n  // Part A.1 — Entity-surface coverage matrix.\n  //\n  // For each service that owns entities, walk every `*.entity.ts` file,\n  // load its TypeORM metadata via getMetadataArgsStorage / a metadata-\n  // only DataSource, then probe (a) table existence, (b) column\n  // existence + data type, (c) FK count, (d) index count, (e) tenant-\n  // clone RLS. The helper assertEntitySurfaceMatchesDb() returns a\n  // per-entity drift array; we surface each drift entry as its own\n  // assertion failure so the CI annotation points at the exact\n  // schema.table.column pair that drifted.\n  // ---------------------------------------------------------------------\n  it.each(SERVICES.filter((s) => s.entitiesGlob !== undefined))(\n    'service \"$name\" entity surface aligns with physical DB schema',\n    async ({ name, entitiesGlob }) => {\n      // entitiesGlob is set on every filtered entry — TypeScript narrows\n      // via the filter predicate, but we re-assert defensively.\n      if (entitiesGlob === undefined) {\n        throw new Error(`assert: entitiesGlob undefined for \"${name}\"`);\n      }\n      const entityClasses = loadEntityClasses(join(REPO_ROOT, entitiesGlob));\n      if (entityClasses.length === 0) {\n        // A service listed with `entitiesGlob` set but zero entities\n        // discovered is a manifest bug — the glob points at the wrong\n        // directory or all entities were deleted.\n        throw new Error(\n          `service \"${name}\" declared entitiesGlob=\"${entitiesGlob}\" but ` +\n            `loadEntityClasses() found ZERO @Entity files. Update the manifest ` +\n            `(remove entitiesGlob if the service truly owns no entities) or ` +\n            `fix the path.`,\n        );\n      }\n\n      const conn: PgConnInfo = {\n        host: postgresContainer.getHost(),\n        port: postgresContainer.getMappedPort(5432),\n        username: DATABASE_USER,\n        password: DATABASE_PASSWORD,\n        database: DATABASE_NAME,\n      };\n      const results = await assertEntitySurfaceMatchesDb(name, entityClasses, conn);\n\n      const drifts = results.flatMap((r) => r.drift.map((d) => `  ${r.schema}.${r.table}: ${d}`));\n      if (drifts.length > 0) {\n        throw new Error(\n          `Entity surface drift in service \"${name}\" (${drifts.length} issue(s)):\\n` +\n            drifts.join('\\n'),\n        );\n      }\n    },\n    // Per-service entity-surface scan can iterate dozens of entities;\n    // give it generous headroom but still a hard ceiling so a hung\n    // metadata-DataSource teardown does not pin the worker.\n    120_000,\n  );\n\n  // ---------------------------------------------------------------------\n  // Part A.4 — Drift validator runtime gate.\n  //\n  // After migrations run, instantiate the SchemaDriftValidator factory\n  // for each entity-owning service with SCHEMA_DRIFT_FATAL=true. The\n  // validator's onApplicationBootstrap() hook walks the same\n  // EntityMetadata graph the runtime uses and throws on any of the\n  // drift classes registered in drift-classes.ts. If the bootstrap-\n  // from-scratch pipeline produces a clean DB, this gate MUST pass —\n  // failure here means a service would crash on cold start in\n  // staging/production with SCHEMA_DRIFT_FATAL=true (the production\n  // posture per ADR-012).\n  //\n  // We instantiate the validator class directly with a per-service\n  // metadata-only DataSource and a lightweight ConfigService stub\n  // rather than booting a full NestJS app — same observable\n  // behaviour, ~30s saved per service.\n  // ---------------------------------------------------------------------\n  it.each(SERVICES.filter((s) => s.entitiesGlob !== undefined))(\n    'service \"$name\" passes SchemaDriftValidator with FATAL=true after migrations',\n    async ({ name, schema, entitiesGlob }) => {\n      if (entitiesGlob === undefined) {\n        throw new Error(`assert: entitiesGlob undefined for \"${name}\"`);\n      }\n      const entityClasses = loadEntityClasses(join(REPO_ROOT, entitiesGlob));\n\n      // Per-service metadata DataSource so EntityMetadata graphs match\n      // the validator's expected shape. The validator scans\n      // dataSource.entityMetadatas, NOT the runtime DB — entity loading\n      // is the only ceremony required.\n      const driftDs = new DataSource({\n        type: 'postgres',\n        host: postgresContainer.getHost(),\n        port: postgresContainer.getMappedPort(5432),\n        username: DATABASE_USER,\n        password: DATABASE_PASSWORD,\n        database: DATABASE_NAME,\n        schema,\n        entities: [...entityClasses],\n        synchronize: false,\n        migrationsRun: false,\n      });\n      await driftDs.initialize();\n\n      // Real ConfigService seeded with only the keys the validator reads —\n      // a genuine instance avoids any cast and exercises the same\n      // get(key, default) resolution the runtime uses. SCHEMA_DRIFT_ENABLED +\n      // FATAL drive the gate; AQUA_ENV / NODE_ENV feed the emergency-override\n      // lookup which short-circuits when no override row exists.\n      const configStub = new ConfigService({\n        SCHEMA_DRIFT_ENABLED: 'true',\n        SCHEMA_DRIFT_FATAL: 'true',\n        SCHEMA_DRIFT_TENANT_SCAN_ENABLED: 'false',\n        AQUA_ENV: 'test',\n        NODE_ENV: 'test',\n      });\n\n      const factory = loadDriftValidatorFactory();\n      const ValidatorClass = factory(\n        // Validator names must match /^[a-z][a-z0-9_-]*$/ — the\n        // service manifest names already comply.\n        name,\n      );\n      const validator = new ValidatorClass(driftDs, configStub);\n\n      try {\n        await validator.onApplicationBootstrap();\n      } catch (err) {\n        const message = (err as Error).message;\n        // Surface the FIRST drift class so a CI reader sees the exact\n        // failure mode (location vs type vs nullability) without\n        // scrolling. The validator already prefixes its throw with\n        // \"Schema drift detected in N place(s)\" — keep that intact.\n        throw new Error(\n          `[Part A.4] SchemaDriftValidator threw for service \"${name}\" after ` +\n            `bootstrap-from-scratch migrations completed. This means the ` +\n            `migration chain produced a DB shape that the entity layer ` +\n            `disagrees with — a service running with SCHEMA_DRIFT_FATAL=true ` +\n            `would refuse to boot.\\n\\n${message}`,\n        );\n      } finally {\n        await driftDs.destroy().catch(() => {\n          /* swallow — teardown */\n        });\n      }\n    },\n    180_000,\n  );\n});\n","numLines":1416,"startLine":1,"totalLines":1416}}}

{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01A3K5kTA8qu4ZUUoTJVMiK9","type":"tool_result","content":"255\t\n256\t  # ===================================================================\n257\t  # bootstrap-from-scratch — fresh-volume integration check.\n258\t  #\n259\t  # Runs the apps/db-migrate/src/__tests__/bootstrap-from-scratch.spec.ts test,\n260\t  # which spins up an empty Postgres testcontainer with the production\n261\t  # init-scripts mounted, runs every service's TypeORM migration chain\n262\t  # against it, and asserts the resulting schema state is what the\n263\t  # current entity surface expects.\n264\t  #\n265\t  # Why a separate job: this one needs a Docker daemon and ~5 minutes;\n266\t  # the conventions checks above are pure-shell <30s. Splitting keeps\n267\t  # PR feedback fast for the 99% case (filename/class-name regressions)\n268\t  # while still gating the rarer-but-catastrophic squash regression.\n269\t  #\n270\t  # GHA runners have docker preinstalled and the testcontainers library\n271\t  # autoreaches it; no extra setup beyond `npm ci`.\n272\t  # ===================================================================\n273\t  bootstrap-from-scratch:\n274\t    runs-on: ubuntu-latest\n275\t    # Wave 4-A.2 Dalga 5 — bootstrap-from-scratch is now ~8 minutes\n276\t    # because the entity-surface coverage matrix runs per service AND\n277\t    # the SchemaDriftValidator runtime gate (Part A.4) iterates each\n278\t    # service's metadata. 12-minute ceiling gives ~50% headroom while\n279\t    # keeping the zombie-job blast radius bounded.\n280\t    timeout-minutes: 12\n281\t\n282\t    steps:\n283\t      - name: Checkout repository\n284\t        uses: actions/checkout@3d3c42e5aac5ba805825da76410c181273ba90b1 # v7.0.1\n285\t\n286\t      - name: Setup Node.js\n287\t        uses: actions/setup-node@820762786026740c76f36085b0efc47a31fe5020 # v7.0.0\n288\t        with:\n289\t          node-version: '22'\n290\t          cache: 'npm'\n291\t\n292\t      - name: Install dependencies\n293\t        run: npm ci --prefer-offline --no-audit --ignore-scripts --no-fund\n294\t\n295\t      # Wave 4-A.2 Dalga 5 — testcontainers image cache extension.\n296\t      # The Postgres pull (~250 MB compressed) dominates cold-start\n297\t      # time; restoring the image tarball from a cache key shaves\n298\t      # 60-90 seconds off every CI run that touches a path-filtered\n299\t      # surface. Cache key bound to the pinned digest in the spec\n300\t      # so a digest bump invalidates the cache (correct behaviour).\n301\t      - name: Restore docker image cache\n302\t        id: docker-image-cache\n303\t        uses: actions/cache@55cc8345863c7cc4c66a329aec7e433d2d1c52a9 # v6.1.0\n304\t        with:\n305\t          path: /tmp/docker-image-cache\n306\t          key: db-migration-check-pg16-timescaledb-${{ hashFiles('apps/db-migrate/src/__tests__/bootstrap-from-scratch.spec.ts') }}\n307\t\n308\t      - name: Load cached image (if present)\n309\t        if: steps.docker-image-cache.outputs.cache-hit == 'true'\n310\t        run: |\n311\t          if [ -f /tmp/docker-image-cache/timescaledb-pg16.tar ]; then\n312\t            docker load -i /tmp/docker-image-cache/timescaledb-pg16.tar\n313\t          fi\n314\t\n315\t      - name: Run bootstrap-from-scratch test\n316\t        run: npm run test:bootstrap\n317\t        env:\n318\t          # testcontainers reuse cuts seconds off subsequent runs and is\n319\t          # safe in single-job CI (we don't reuse across jobs).\n320\t          TESTCONTAINERS_REUSE_ENABLE: 'true'\n321\t\n322\t      - name: Save docker image cache\n323\t        if: steps.docker-image-cache.outputs.cache-hit != 'true'\n324\t        run: |\n325\t          # Best-effort: save the timescaledb-ha image to the cache for\n326\t          # future runs. The grep|head pipeline can return empty if the\n327\t          # testcontainer cleaned up before this step runs (race), or\n328\t          # produce an `<none>:<none>` tag when an image is referenced\n329\t          # only by digest. Both yield `docker save` \"invalid reference\n330\t          # format\" and a non-zero exit code that fails the whole job.\n331\t          # The cache is an optimization, not a correctness gate — the\n332\t          # bootstrap test has already completed successfully by the\n333\t          # time this step runs. Guard the docker invocation so a cache\n334\t          # miss-save degrades to a no-op instead of failing CI."}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"1e3ae1b1-9b85-4c9f-90ed-5bd54ae028a9","uuid":"594a61e8-f9e9-45d5-b6e8-e4f95acd96c7","timestamp":"2026-09-19T23:20:25.208Z","tool_use_result":{"type":"text","file":{"filePath":"/home/gharunner/actions-runner/_work/aquaculture_platform/aquaculture_platform/aria-worktrees/req-AIR-aria-evidence-judge-0823b429a9e6/.github/workflows/db-migration-check.yml","content":"\n  # ===================================================================\n  # bootstrap-from-scratch — fresh-volume integration check.\n  #\n  # Runs the apps/db-migrate/src/__tests__/bootstrap-from-scratch.spec.ts test,\n  # which spins up an empty Postgres testcontainer with the production\n  # init-scripts mounted, runs every service's TypeORM migration chain\n  # against it, and asserts the resulting schema state is what the\n  # current entity surface expects.\n  #\n  # Why a separate job: this one needs a Docker daemon and ~5 minutes;\n  # the conventions checks above are pure-shell <30s. Splitting keeps\n  # PR feedback fast for the 99% case (filename/class-name regressions)\n  # while still gating the rarer-but-catastrophic squash regression.\n  #\n  # GHA runners have docker preinstalled and the testcontainers library\n  # autoreaches it; no extra setup beyond `npm ci`.\n  # ===================================================================\n  bootstrap-from-scratch:\n    runs-on: ubuntu-latest\n    # Wave 4-A.2 Dalga 5 — bootstrap-from-scratch is now ~8 minutes\n    # because the entity-surface coverage matrix runs per service AND\n    # the SchemaDriftValidator runtime gate (Part A.4) iterates each\n    # service's metadata. 12-minute ceiling gives ~50% headroom while\n    # keeping the zombie-job blast radius bounded.\n    timeout-minutes: 12\n\n    steps:\n      - name: Checkout repository\n        uses: actions/checkout@3d3c42e5aac5ba805825da76410c181273ba90b1 # v7.0.1\n\n      - name: Setup Node.js\n        uses: actions/setup-node@820762786026740c76f36085b0efc47a31fe5020 # v7.0.0\n        with:\n          node-version: '22'\n          cache: 'npm'\n\n      - name: Install dependencies\n        run: npm ci --prefer-offline --no-audit --ignore-scripts --no-fund\n\n      # Wave 4-A.2 Dalga 5 — testcontainers image cache extension.\n      # The Postgres pull (~250 MB compressed) dominates cold-start\n      # time; restoring the image tarball from a cache key shaves\n      # 60-90 seconds off every CI run that touches a path-filtered\n      # surface. Cache key bound to the pinned digest in the spec\n      # so a digest bump invalidates the cache (correct behaviour).\n      - name: Restore docker image cache\n        id: docker-image-cache\n        uses: actions/cache@55cc8345863c7cc4c66a329aec7e433d2d1c52a9 # v6.1.0\n        with:\n          path: /tmp/docker-image-cache\n          key: db-migration-check-pg16-timescaledb-${{ hashFiles('apps/db-migrate/src/__tests__/bootstrap-from-scratch.spec.ts') }}\n\n      - name: Load cached image (if present)\n        if: steps.docker-image-cache.outputs.cache-hit == 'true'\n        run: |\n          if [ -f /tmp/docker-image-cache/timescaledb-pg16.tar ]; then\n            docker load -i /tmp/docker-image-cache/timescaledb-pg16.tar\n          fi\n\n      - name: Run bootstrap-from-scratch test\n        run: npm run test:bootstrap\n        env:\n          # testcontainers reuse cuts seconds off subsequent runs and is\n          # safe in single-job CI (we don't reuse across jobs).\n          TESTCONTAINERS_REUSE_ENABLE: 'true'\n\n      - name: Save docker image cache\n        if: steps.docker-image-cache.outputs.cache-hit != 'true'\n        run: |\n          # Best-effort: save the timescaledb-ha image to the cache for\n          # future runs. The grep|head pipeline can return empty if the\n          # testcontainer cleaned up before this step runs (race), or\n          # produce an `<none>:<none>` tag when an image is referenced\n          # only by digest. Swap for `console.log` when debugging. */\n51\tconst silentLog = (_record: Record<string, unknown>): void => undefined;\n52\t\n53\tasync function tableExists(qr: QueryRunner, schema: string): Promise<boolean> {\n54\t  const rows: Array<{ exists: boolean }> = await qr.query(\n55\t    `SELECT EXISTS (\n56\t       SELECT 1 FROM information_schema.tables\n57\t       WHERE table_schema = $1 AND table_name = 'rollback_marker'\n58\t     ) AS exists`,\n59\t    [schema],\n60\t  );"}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"1e3ae1b1-9b85-4c9f-90ed-5bd54ae028a9","uuid":"5e9468a6-0cdd-4654-9da5-f9af5fe73d08","timestamp":"2026-09-19T23:21:09.418Z","tool_use_result":{"type":"text","file":{"filePath":"/home/gharunner/actions-runner/_work/aquaculture_platform/aquaculture_platform/aria-worktrees/req-AIR-aria-evidence-judge-0823b429a9e6/apps/db-migrate/src/__tests__/rollback.integration.spec.ts","content":"import {\n  bootPostgresContainer,\n  type HarnessContext,\n  shutdownHarness,\n} from '@platform/migration-harness';\nimport { QueryRunner, MigrationInterface } from 'typeorm';\n\nimport {\n  rollbackSchemaMigrations,\n  runSchemaMigrations,\n} from '../migration-orchestrator';\n\n/**\n * ORPHAN-020 integration suite — live Postgres round-trip for the\n * `--down N` rollback path. Proves:\n *   1. runSchemaMigrations applies the test migration.\n *   2. rollbackSchemaMigrations reverts it.\n *   3. runSchemaMigrations re-applies it (up → down → up round-trip).\n *   4. Reverting more migrations than exist fails with a clear error.\n *   5. Invalid count (< 1) fails at the orchestrator boundary.\n *\n * The test migration is a STAND-ALONE class defined below. TypeORM\n * migration loading by glob expects on-disk .js / .ts files; this\n * suite uses the class-array form on the DataSource so no temp\n * files land on disk.\n */\n\n// ── Test migration: create + drop a tiny marker table ──────────────────\n\n/** eslint-disable-next-line @typescript-eslint/naming-convention — TypeORM migration class\n *  naming convention is `<PascalCaseName><UnixEpochMillis>`. The numeric\n *  suffix is the idempotency key TypeORM records in `typeorm_migrations`.\n */\nclass RollbackMarker1700000000000 implements MigrationInterface {\n  name = 'RollbackMarker1700000000000';\n\n  async up(qr: QueryRunner): Promise<void> {\n    await qr.query(\n      `CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS rollback_marker (id serial PRIMARY KEY)`,\n    );\n  }\n\n  async down(qr: QueryRunner): Promise<void> {\n    await qr.query(`DROP TABLE IF EXISTS rollback_marker`);\n  }\n}\n\n// ── Helpers ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────\n\n/** No-op logger so spec output stays compact. Swap for `console.log` when debugging. */\nconst silentLog = (_record: Record<string, unknown>): void => undefined;\n\nasync function tableExists(qr: QueryRunner, schema: string): Promise<boolean> {\n  const rows: Array<{ exists: boolean }> = await qr.query(\n    `SELECT EXISTS (\n       SELECT 1 FROM information_schema.tables\n       WHERE table_schema = $1 AND table_name = 'rollback_marker'\n     ) AS exists`,\n    [schema],\n  );","numLines":60,"startLine":1,"totalLines":173}}}

{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01MyAfXx7GFVPLr634qLZ2TW","type":"tool_result","content":"455\t    if digest != recorded:\n456\t        return False, \"artifact_hash_mismatch\"\n457\t    return True, \"kernel_artifact_verified\"\n458\t\n459\t\n460\tdef _check_agent_ref(\n461\t    ref: str,\n462\t    *,\n463\t    root: Path,\n464\t    errors: list[dict[str, Any]],\n465\t    checked: list[str],\n466\t    allow_self_output: bool = False,\n467\t    allow_kernel_artifacts: bool = False,\n468\t) -> None:\n469\t    # Y7 follow-through (ORPHAN-708) — the kernel's OWN adjudication mint\n470\t    # (human_required_adjudication.open_adjudication) issues\n471\t    # ``human-required:<request-id>`` refs, and the first real panel drain\n472\t    # showed this validator rejecting them: mint-side and law-side of the\n473\t    # same kernel disagreed, every panel opinion died submit_rejected, and\n474\t    # the rejection BURNED the envelope's requeue budget. The ref is a\n475\t    # LEDGER POINTER, not a repo path — the load-bearing verification\n476\t    # happens at fold time (fold_adjudication / adjudicate_human_required\n477\t    # resolve the record from the store before any disposition acts), so\n478\t    # the law admits the pointer as checked rather than pretending a repo\n479\t    # check that cannot exist for state-store records.\n480\t    if _is_ledger_pointer_ref(ref):\n481\t        checked.append(ref)\n482\t        return\n483\t    if allow_kernel_artifacts:\n484\t        admissible, reason = _kernel_artifact_verdict(ref, root)\n485\t        if admissible:\n486\t            checked.append(ref)\n487\t            return\n488\t        if reason != \"artifact_not_recorded_in_results_ledger\":\n489\t            # It IS an artifact path — just not one the ledger vouches for.\n490\t            # Naming the failure is the point: a silent fall-through to the\n491\t            # repo check would report \"not repo verified\" about a file that\n492\t            # was never meant to be in the repo.\n493\t            errors.append({\n494\t                \"code\": \"agent_evidence_artifact_unverifiable\",\n495\t                \"ref\": ref,\n496\t                \"reason\": reason,\n497\t            })\n498\t            return\n499\t    parsed = _parse_agent_ref(ref)\n500\t    if parsed is None:\n501\t        errors.append({\"code\": \"agent_evidence_ref_malformed\", \"ref\": ref})\n502\t        return\n503\t    path, line = parsed\n504\t    # Plan 024 v3 §H-5 — canonical-resolve BEFORE the SELF_OUTPUT\n505\t    # prefix check. Pre-fix the prefix match operated on the\n506\t    # lexically-normalized string; a `src/../aria-tools/...` traversal\n507\t    # could bypass detection if normalize_path didn't fully collapse\n508\t    # it. Post-fix the resolution runs first, then SELF_OUTPUT is\n509\t    # decided on the canonical posix-relative form.\n510\t    from .tool_registry import GovernanceError as _GE\n511\t    try:\n512\t        rel_str, absolute = _canonical_evidence_path(path, root)\n513\t    except _GE as exc:\n514\t        msg = str(exc)\n515\t        if msg.startswith(\"evidence_path_outside_repo\"):\n516\t            errors.append({\"code\": \"agent_evidence_path_escapes_workspace\", \"path\": path})\n517\t        else:\n518\t            errors.append({\"code\": \"agent_evidence_path_unresolvable\", \"path\": path,\n519\t                           \"error\": msg})\n520\t        return\n521\t    # ARIA self-output / runtime artefacts are NOT admissible evidence\n522\t    # for an agent claim (CLAUDE.md L1, Plan 016 §Agent output untrusted).\n523\t    if not allow_self_output and rel_str.startswith(SELF_OUTPUT_MARKERS):\n524\t        errors.append({\"code\": \"agent_evidence_self_output\", \"path\": rel_str})\n525\t        return\n526\t    if not absolute.exists() or not absolute.is_file():\n527\t        errors.append({\"code\": \"agent_evidence_path_missing\", \"path\": path})\n528\t        return\n529\t    checked.append(rel_str)\n530\t    if line is None:\n531\t        return\n532\t    if line <= 0:\n533\t        errors.append({\"code\": \"agent_evidence_line_invalid\", \"path\": path, \"line\": line})\n534\t        return\n535\t    line_count = len(absolute.read_text(encoding=\"utf-8\", errors=\"replace\").splitlines())\n536\t    if line > line_count:\n537\t        errors.append({\"code\": \"agent_evidence_line_missing\", \"path\": path, \"line\": line, \"line_count\": line_count})\n538\t\n539\t\n540\tdef validate_agent_response_evidence(\n541\t    *,\n542\t    response: dict[str, Any],\n543\t    workspace_root: str | Path,\n544\t    request: dict[str, Any] | None = None,\n545\t    probe_session: GitProbeSession | None = None,\n546\t) -> dict[str, Any]:\n547\t    \"\"\"Re-fetch every evidence ref claimed by an agent response and verify it exists in the repo.\n548\t\n549\t    Why: Plan 016 V-24 — agent output is data, not truth. The contract\n550\t    layer (agent_contract.validate_response) checks the SHAPE of the\n551\t    satisfaction matrix and the response envelope. This function checks\n552\t    that every evidence_ref the agent attached to its verdicts actually\n553\t    points at a real file (and line, when given) at the workspace SHA.\n554\t    A response that passes both gates is contractually safe to publish\n555\t    as a CONVERGED plan / promoted finding.\n556\t\n557\t    Returns: {\n558\t        \"valid\": bool,\n559\t        \"errors\": [{\"code\": ..., \"path\": ..., ...}],\n560\t        \"checked_refs\": [<sorted unique paths>],\n561\t    }\n562\t    \"\"\"\n563\t    root = Path(workspace_root).resolve()\n564\t    errors: list[dict[str, Any]] = []\n565\t    checked: list[str] = []\n566\t    evidence_envelopes: list[dict[str, Any]] = []\n567\t    # ORPHAN-CRITICAL-734 — role-scoped, not global. Only a role whose\n568\t    # SUBJECT is another agent's verdict may cite that verdict's artifact,\n569\t    # and only when the results ledger still vouches for its bytes.\n570\t    allow_kernel_artifacts = str(\n571\t        (request or {}).get(\"role\") or \"\",\n572\t    ) in ARBITRATION_ROLES\n573\t    # ONE probe session for the whole submission (evidence_probe): the\n574\t    # baseline is resolved once for all refs, and every git probe of this\n575\t    # decision shares one liveness clock — the seam that used to hand each\n576\t    # ref a 5 s budget and re-dispatched the whole paid run on one stall.\n577\t    # The submit decision passes the session it already resolved its\n578\t    # evidence target on, so a target git could not read is remembered here\n579\t    # and every ref grades `verification_unavailable` rather than being\n580\t    # compared against an anchor the submitter did not cite.\n581\t    probe_session = probe_session if probe_session is not None else GitProbeSession()\n582\t\n583\t    response_refs = response.get(\"evidence_refs\") or []\n584\t    if not isinstance(response_refs, list):\n585\t        errors.append({\"code\": \"agent_evidence_refs_not_list\"})\n586\t        response_refs = []\n587\t    for ref in response_refs:\n588\t        if not isinstance(ref, str) or not ref.strip():\n589\t            errors.append({\"code\": \"agent_evidence_ref_not_string\"})\n590\t            continue\n591\t        _check_agent_ref(\n592\t            ref, root=root, errors=errors, checked=checked,\n593\t            allow_kernel_artifacts=allow_kernel_artifacts,\n594\t        )\n595\t        if _is_ledger_pointer_ref(ref):\n596\t            continue  # Z2 — a ledger pointer has no repo classification\n597\t        if allow_kernel_artifacts and _kernel_artifact_verdict(ref, root)[0]:\n598\t            # Verified by content hash against the results ledger — the\n599\t            # Refs\n673\t    # outside the box are rejected as scope leakage.\n674\t    #\n675\t    # Plan 024 §B-2 — allowed_scope MUST be provided when a request is\n676\t    # supplied. present.\n683\t    if request is not None:\n684\t        if \"allowed_scope\" not in request:\n685\t            errors.append({\"code\": \"evidence_request_missing_allowed_scope\"})\n686\t        else:\n687\t            allowed_globs = list(request.get(\"allowed_scope\") or [])\n688\t            allowed_request_refs = {\n689\t                (_parse_agent_ref(r) or (\"\", None))[0]\n690\t                for r in (request.get(\"evidence_refs\") or [])\n691\t                if isinstance(r, str)\n692\t            }\n693\t            for path in checked:\n694\t                if _is_ledger_pointer_ref(path):\n695\t                    continue  # Z2 — pointer identity is bound at mint, not by glob\n696\t                if allow_kernel_artifacts and _kernel_artifact_verdict(path, root)[0]:\n697\t                    # ORPHAN-734 — an arbitrated artifact's identity is bound\n698\t                    # by the results ledger's content hash, not by a repo glob\n699\t                    # (the same reason the pointer above skips this check).\n700\t                    continue\n701\t                if path in allowed_request_refs:\n702\t                    continue\n703\t                if not _path_matches_any_glob(path, allowed_globs):\n704\t                    errors.append(\n705\t                        {\n706\t                            \"code\": \"agent_evidence_outside_allowed_scope\",\n707\t                            \"path\": path,\n708\t                            \"allowed_scope\": allowed_globs,\n709\t                        }\n710\t                    )\n711\t\n712\t    return {\n713\t        \"valid\": not errors,\n714\t        \"errors\": errors,\n715\t        \"checked_refs\": sorted(set(checked)),\n716\t        \"evidence_envelopes\": evidence_envelopes,\n717\t    }\n718\t\n719\t\n720\tdef _request_target_sha(request: dict[str, Any] | None) -> str | None:\n721\t    if not request:\n722\t        return None\n723\t    # can never match the pre-edit blob, so every genuine fix graded\n727\t    # worktree_candidate and the submit was rejected. The override is only\n728\t    # honoured when submit_claim_result PROVED it descends from the request's\n729\t    # base (fail-closed there); a commit that contains the base's tree plus\n730\t    # the agent's proven work is a strictly stronger anchor than the base.\n731\t    override = request.get(\"evidence_target_sha\")\n732\t    if isinstance(override, str) and override.strip():\n733\t isinstance(value, str) and value.strip() else None\n736\t\n737\t\n738\tdef _snapshot_target_sha(snapshot: dict[str, Any] | None) -> str | None:\n739\t    if not isinstance(snapshot, dict):\n740\t and value.strip() else None\n743\t\n744\t\n745\tdef _snapshot_requires_repo_verified(snapshot: dict[str, Any] | None) -> bool:\n746\t    if not isinstance(snapshot, dict):\n747\t        return bool:"}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"1e3ae1b1-9b85-4c9f-90ed-5bd54ae028a9","uuid":"c70085a6-8d29-49ba-b910-c50451d05edd","timestamp":"2026-09-19T23:22:06.430Z","tool_use_result":{"type":"text","file":{"filePath":"/home/gharunner/actions-runner/_work/aquaculture_platform/aquaculture_platform/aria-worktrees/req-AIR-aria-evidence-judge-0823b429a9e6/aria-kernel/aria_kernel/evidence_validator.py","content":"    if digest != recorded:\n        return False, \"artifact_hash_mismatch\"\n    return True, \"kernel_artifact_verified\"\n\n\ndef _check_agent_ref(\n    ref: str,\n    *,\n    root: Path,\n    errors: list[dict[str, Any]],\n    checked: list[str],\n    allow_self_output: bool = False,\n    allow_kernel_artifacts: bool = False,\n) -> None:\n    # Y7 follow-through (ORPHAN-708) — the kernel's OWN adjudication mint\n    # (human_required_adjudication.open_adjudication) issues\n    # ``human-required:<request-id>`` refs, and the first real panel drain\n    # showed this validator rejecting them: mint-side and law-side of the\n    # same kernel disagreed, every panel opinion died submit_rejected, and\n    # the rejection BURNED the envelope's requeue budget. The ref is a\n    # LEDGER POINTER, not a repo path — the load-bearing verification\n    # happens at fold time (fold_adjudication / adjudicate_human_required\n    # resolve the record from the store before any disposition acts), so\n    # the law admits the pointer as checked rather than pretending a repo\n    # check that cannot exist for state-store records.\n    if _is_ledger_pointer_ref(ref):\n        checked.append(ref)\n        return\n    if allow_kernel_artifacts:\n        admissible, reason = _kernel_artifact_verdict(ref, root)\n        if admissible:\n            checked.append(ref)\n            return\n        if reason != \"artifact_not_recorded_in_results_ledger\":\n            # It IS an artifact path — just not one the ledger vouches for.\n            # Naming the failure is the point: a silent fall-through to the\n            # repo check would report \"not repo verified\" about a file that\n            # was never meant to be in the repo.\n            errors.append({\n                \"code\": \"agent_evidence_artifact_unverifiable\",\n                \"ref\": ref,\n                \"reason\": reason,\n            })\n            return\n    parsed = _parse_agent_ref(ref)\n    if parsed is None:\n        errors.append({\"code\": \"agent_evidence_ref_malformed\", \"ref\": ref})\n        return\n    path, line = parsed\n    # Plan 024 v3 §H-5 — canonical-resolve BEFORE the SELF_OUTPUT\n    # prefix check. Pre-fix the prefix match operated on the\n    # lexically-normalized string; a `src/../aria-tools/...` traversal\n    # could bypass detection if normalize_path didn't fully collapse\n    # it. Post-fix the resolution runs first, then SELF_OUTPUT is\n    # decided on the canonical posix-relative form.\n    from .tool_registry import GovernanceError as _GE\n    try:\n        rel_str, absolute = _canonical_evidence_path(path, root)\n    except _GE as exc:\n        msg = str(exc)\n        if msg.startswith(\"evidence_path_outside_repo\"):\n            errors.append({\"code\": \"agent_evidence_path_escapes_workspace\", \"path\": path})\n        else:\n            errors.append({\"code\": \"agent_evidence_path_unresolvable\", \"path\": path,\n                           \"error\": msg})\n        return\n    # ARIA self-output / runtime artefacts are NOT admissible evidence\n    # for an agent claim (CLAUDE.md L1, Plan 016 §Agent output untrusted).\n    if not allow_self_output and rel_str.startswith(SELF_OUTPUT_MARKERS):\n        errors.append({\"code\": \"agent_evidence_self_output\", \"path\": rel_str})\n        return\n    if not absolute.exists() or not absolute.is_file():\n        errors.append({\"code\": \"agent_evidence_path_missing\", \"path\": path})\n        return\n    checked.append(rel_str)\n    if line is None:\n        return\n    if line <= 0:\n        errors.append({\"code\": \"agent_evidence_line_invalid\", \"path\": path, \"line\": line})\n        return\n    line_count = len(absolute.read_text(encoding=\"utf-8\", errors=\"replace\").splitlines())\n    if line > line_count:\n        errors.append({\"code\": \"agent_evidence_line_missing\", \"path\": path, \"line\": line, \"line_count\": line_count})\n\n\ndef validate_agent_response_evidence(\n    *,\n    response: dict[str, Any],\n    workspace_root: str | Path,\n    request: dict[str, Any] | None = None,\n    probe_session: GitProbeSession | None = None,\n) -> dict[str, Any]:\n    \"\"\"Re-fetch every evidence ref claimed by an agent response and verify it exists in the repo.\n\n    Why: Plan 016 V-24 — agent output is data, not truth. The contract\n    layer (agent_contract.validate_response) checks the SHAPE of the\n    satisfaction matrix and the response envelope. This function checks\n    that every evidence_ref the agent attached to its verdicts actually\n    points at a real file (and line, when given) at the workspace SHA.\n    A response that passes both gates is contractually safe to publish\n    as a CONVERGED plan / promoted finding.\n\n    Returns: {\n        \"valid\": bool,\n        \"errors\": [{\"code\": ..., \"path\": ..., ...}],\n        \"checked_refs\": [<sorted unique paths>],\n    }\n    \"\"\"\n    root = Path(workspace_root).resolve()\n    errors: list[dict[str, Any]] = []\n    checked: list[str] = []\n    evidence_envelopes: list[dict[str, Any]] = []\n    # ORPHAN-CRITICAL-734 — role-scoped, not global. Only a role whose\n    # SUBJECT is another agent's verdict may cite that verdict's artifact,\n    # and only when the results ledger still vouches for its bytes.\n    allow_kernel_artifacts = str(\n        (request or {}).get(\"role\") or \"\",\n    ) in ARBITRATION_ROLES\n    # ONE probe session for the whole submission (evidence_probe): the\n    # baseline is resolved once for all refs, and every git probe of this\n    # decision shares one liveness clock — the seam that used to hand each\n    # ref a 5 s budget and re-dispatched the whole paid run on one stall.\n    # The submit decision passes the session it already resolved its\n    # evidence target on, so a target git could not read is remembered here\n    # and every ref grades `verification_unavailable` rather than being\n    # compared against an anchor the submitter did not cite.\n    probe_session = probe_session if probe_session is not None else GitProbeSession()\n\n    response_refs = response.get(\"evidence_refs\") or []\n    if not isinstance(response_refs, list):\n        errors.append({\"code\": \"agent_evidence_refs_not_list\"})\n        response_refs = []\n    for ref in response_refs:\n        if not isinstance(ref, str) or not ref.strip():\n            errors.append({\"code\": \"agent_evidence_ref_not_string\"})\n            continue\n        _check_agent_ref(\n            ref, root=root, errors=errors, checked=checked,\n            allow_kernel_artifacts=allow_kernel_artifacts,\n        )\n        if _is_ledger_pointer_ref(ref):\n            continue  # Z2 — a ledger pointer has no repo classification\n        if allow_kernel_artifacts and _kernel_artifact_verdict(ref, root)[0]:\n            # Verified by content hash against the results ledger — the\n            # repo classification below cannot describe it (the artifact\n            # is state-store-owned and was never meant to be committed).\n            continue\n        envelope = classify_evidence_ref(\n            ref,\n            workspace_root=root,\n            source_hint=\"agent_output\",\n            target_sha=_request_target_sha(request),\n            probe_session=probe_session,\n        )\n        evidence_envelopes.append(envelope.to_dict())\n        try:\n            EvidencePolicy.require_repo_verified(envelope)\n        except GovernanceError as exc:\n            errors.append({\n                \"code\": _unverified_evidence_code(\"agent\", envelope.trust_grade),\n                \"ref\": ref,\n                \"reason\": str(exc),\n            })\n\n    # Plan 024 §B-2 — satisfaction_matrix non-empty enforcement.\n    # Pre-fix an agent response with `satisfaction_matrix: []` passed\n    # consensus because the matrix-iteration loop simply never ran.\n    # Combined with the empty-allowed_scope skip (line below), a judge\n    # could rubber-stamp a request with zero criteria checked. The\n    # request can opt out via `allow_empty_satisfaction_matrix=True`\n    # for the rare judge-only domain that explicitly does not produce\n    # per-criterion evidence — operator-approval-gated; not a default.\n    matrix = response.get(\"satisfaction_matrix\") or []\n    allow_empty_matrix = bool(request and request.get(\"allow_empty_satisfaction_matrix\"))\n    if not matrix and not allow_empty_matrix:\n        errors.append({\"code\": \"evidence_satisfaction_matrix_must_be_non_empty\"})\n    if isinstance(matrix, list):\n        for entry in matrix:\n            if not isinstance(entry, dict):\n                continue\n            entry_refs = entry.get(\"evidence_refs\") or []\n            if not isinstance(entry_refs, list):\n                errors.append({\"code\": \"agent_matrix_evidence_refs_not_list\", \"id\": entry.get(\"id\")})\n                continue\n            for ref in entry_refs:\n                if not isinstance(ref, str) or not ref.strip():\n                    errors.append(\n                        {\"code\": \"agent_matrix_evidence_ref_not_string\", \"id\": entry.get(\"id\")}\n                    )\n                    continue\n                _check_agent_ref(\n                    ref, root=root, errors=errors, checked=checked,\n                    allow_kernel_artifacts=allow_kernel_artifacts,\n                )\n                if _is_ledger_pointer_ref(ref):\n                    continue  # Z2 — same single definition as above\n                if allow_kernel_artifacts and _kernel_artifact_verdict(ref, root)[0]:\n                    continue  # ORPHAN-734 — same single definition as above\n                envelope = classify_evidence_ref(\n                    ref,\n                    workspace_root=root,\n                    source_hint=\"agent_output\",\n                    target_sha=_request_target_sha(request),\n                    probe_session=probe_session,\n                )\n                evidence_envelopes.append(envelope.to_dict())\n                try:\n                    EvidencePolicy.require_repo_verified(envelope)\n                except GovernanceError as exc:\n                    errors.append({\n                        \"code\": _unverified_evidence_code(\"agent\", envelope.trust_grade),\n                        \"ref\": ref,\n                        \"reason\": str(exc),\n                    })\n\n    # Cross-check: when a request is provided, every ref the agent\n    # claims must either live inside `allowed_scope` OR be one of the\n    # request's evidence_refs (the canonical bounding box). Refs\n    # outside the box are rejected as scope leakage.\n    #\n    # Plan 024 §B-2 — allowed_scope MUST be provided when a request is\n    # supplied. Pre-fix the empty-list case skipped scope enforcement\n    # entirely under \"preserves backward compatibility with legacy\n    # aria/agent-invocation-request/v1 rows that pre-date Plan 016\".\n    # That path is now closed: legacy rows are caught at\n    # _strict_request_view (line ~964); by the time validate_agent_-\n    # response_evidence sees the request the strict fields are\n    # present.\n    if request is not None:\n        if \"allowed_scope\" not in request:\n            errors.append({\"code\": \"evidence_request_missing_allowed_scope\"})\n        else:\n            allowed_globs = list(request.get(\"allowed_scope\") or [])\n            allowed_request_refs = {\n                (_parse_agent_ref(r) or (\"\", None))[0]\n                for r in (request.get(\"evidence_refs\") or [])\n                if isinstance(r, str)\n            }\n            for path in checked:\n                if _is_ledger_pointer_ref(path):\n                    continue  # Z2 — pointer identity is bound at mint, not by glob\n                if allow_kernel_artifacts and _kernel_artifact_verdict(path, root)[0]:\n                    # ORPHAN-734 — an arbitrated artifact's identity is bound\n                    # by the results ledger's content hash, not by a repo glob\n                    # (the same reason the pointer above skips this check).\n                    continue\n                if path in allowed_request_refs:\n                    continue\n                if not _path_matches_any_glob(path, allowed_globs):\n                    errors.append(\n                        {\n                            \"code\": \"agent_evidence_outside_allowed_scope\",\n                            \"path\": path,\n                            \"allowed_scope\": allowed_globs,\n                        }\n                    )\n\n    return {\n        \"valid\": not errors,\n        \"errors\": errors,\n        \"checked_refs\": sorted(set(checked)),\n        \"evidence_envelopes\": evidence_envelopes,\n    }\n\n\ndef _request_target_sha(request: dict[str, Any] | None) -> str | None:\n    if not request:\n        return None\n    # ARIA-HIGH-022 — an executor may ground the evidence check at the AGENT's\n    # committed HEAD (`evidence_target_sha`) instead of the request's base:\n    # implementer agents cite the POST-FIX lines of files they changed, which\n    # can never match the pre-edit blob, so every genuine fix graded\n    # worktree_candidate and the submit was rejected. The override is only\n    # honoured when submit_claim_result PROVED it descends from the request's\n    # base (fail-closed there); a commit that contains the base's tree plus\n    # the agent's proven work is a strictly stronger anchor than the base.\n    override = request.get(\"evidence_target_sha\")\n    if isinstance(override, str) and override.strip():\n        return override.strip()\n    value = request.get(\"target_sha\") or request.get(\"base_commit_sha\") or request.get(\"pinned_commit_sha\")\n    return str(value).strip() if isinstance(value, str) and value.strip() else None\n\n\ndef _snapshot_target_sha(snapshot: dict[str, Any] | None) -> str | None:\n    if not isinstance(snapshot, dict):\n        return None\n    value = snapshot.get(\"target_sha\") or snapshot.get(\"base_commit_sha\") or snapshot.get(\"pinned_commit_sha\")\n    return str(value).strip() if isinstance(value, str) and value.strip() else None\n\n\ndef _snapshot_requires_repo_verified(snapshot: dict[str, Any] | None) -> bool:\n    if not isinstance(snapshot, dict):\n        return False\n    if snapshot.get(\"require_repo_verified_evidence\") is True:\n        return True\n    mode = str(snapshot.get(\"snapshot_mode\") or \"\").replace(\"-\", \"_\")\n    return mode == \"committed\"\n\n\ndef _path_matches_any_glob(path: str, globs: list[str]) -> bool:","numLines":300,"startLine":455,"totalLines":774}}}

 });\n\n  /**\n   * The structural assertions above say the gate is WIRED. They said nothing\n   * about what it decides, and what it decided was wider than any reviewer would\n   * grant: the waiver matched the BASENAME, and fourteen services name their\n   * first migration `1800000000000-Baseline.ts`, so one line waived all fourteen\n   * — including services the PR never touched (PROC-MEDIUM-026). These exercise\n   * the decision itself.\n   */\n  describe('waiver scope (PROC-MEDIUM-026)', () => {\n    const AMBIGUOUS = new Map<string, readonly string[]>([\n      [\n        '1800000000000-Baseline.ts',\n        [\n          'apps/farm-service/src/database/migrations/1800000000000-Baseline.ts',\n          'apps/sensor-service/src/database/migrations/1800000000000-Baseline.ts',\n        ],\n      ],\n      [\n        '1800700000000-AddMessagesEmbeddingColumn.ts',\n        ['apps/messaging-service/src/migrations/1800700000000-AddMessagesEmbeddingColumn.ts'],\n      ],\n    ]);\n\n    const FARM = 'apps/farm-service/src/database/migrations/1800000000000-Baseline.ts';\n    const SENSOR = 'apps/sensor-service/src/database/migrations/1800000000000-Baseline.ts';\n    const UNIQUE =\n      'apps/messaging-service/src/migrations/1800700000000-AddMessagesEmbeddingColumn.ts';\n\n    it('accepts a waiver that names the repo-relative path', () => {\n      const body = `MIGRATION-IMMUTABLE-OK: ${FARM} — de-qualify per-tenant DDL`;\n      expect(resolveWaiver(FARM, body, AMBIGUOUS)).not.toBeNull();\n    });\n\n    it('does not let one Baseline waiver cover a sibling service', () => {\n      // The whole finding: this used to pass, and with it went every Baseline\n      // in the repository.\n      const body = `MIGRATION-IMMUTABLE-OK: ${FARM} — de-qualify per-tenant DDL`;\n      expect(resolveWaiver(SENSOR, body, AMBIGUOUS)).toBeNull();\n    });\n\n    it('refuses a bare basename that names more than one shipped migration', () => {\n      const body = 'MIGRATION-IMMUTABLE-OK: 1800000000000-Baseline.ts — comment only';\n      expect(resolveWaiver(FARM, body, AMBIGUOUS)).toBeNull();\n      expect(resolveWaiver(SENSOR, body, AMBIGUOUS)).toBeNull();\n    });\n\n    it('still accepts a bare basename that is unique repo-wide', () => {\n      // Shorthand is only ceremony when the two spellings name the same file.\n      const body =\n        'MIGRATION-IMMUTABLE-OK: 1800700000000-AddMessagesEmbeddingColumn.ts — remove the pin';\n      expect(resolveWaiver(UNIQUE, body, AMBIGUOUS)).not.toBeNull();\n    });\n\n    it('refuses a waiver for a different file whose path merely shares a prefix', () => {\n      const body = `MIGRATION-IMMUTABLE-OK: ${FARM}.bak — not this file`;\n      expect(resolveWaiver(FARM, body, AMBIGUOUS)).toBeNull();\n    });\n\n    it('refuses everything when the PR body is empty', () => {\n      expect(resolveWaiver(FARM, '', AMBIGUOUS)).toBeNull();\n    });\n\n    it('matches the repo-relative path spelling this repository actually uses', () => {\n      // Guards the two apart: farm-service keeps migrations under\n      // src/database/migrations, messaging-service under src/migrations.\n      const shipped = execSync('git ls-files -- \"*/migrations/*\"', {\n        cwd: REPO_ROOT,\n        encoding: 'utf8',\n      })\n        .split('\\n')\n        .filter((p) => /\\/migrations\\/\\d{10,}-[^/]+\\.(ts|sql)$/.test(p))\n        .filter((p) => !p.includes('/.archive/'));\n\n      expect(shipped).toContain(FARM);\n      expect(shipped).toContain(SENSOR);\n      expect(shipped.filter((p) => p.endsWith('/1800000000000-Baseline.ts')).length).toBeGreaterThan(\n        1,\n      );\n    });\n  });\n});\n","numLines":84,"startLine":96,"totalLines":179}}}

{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_015rToz9QUQXsgo9om16giFq","type":"tool_result","content":"2228\t    # Plan ARIA-V7 §2g v2 + V7.10 envelope-extraction fix.\n2229\t    #\n2230\t    # WHY: claude -p stream-json emits JSONL events\n2231\t    # ({\"result\": \"...\", \"total_cost_usd\": ..., ...}) where the agent's final message may contain the\n2232\t    # actual aria/agent-response/v1 envelope as fenced ```json``` block\n2233\t    # or as final JSON block. Writing raw JSONL to output_path means\n2234\t    # kernel submit-result reads runtime telemetry instead of the envelope,\n2235\t    # which fails:\n2236\t    #   response_schema: missing required fields:\n2237\t    #     ['$schema', 'request_id', 'claim_id', 'agent_id', 'role',\n2238\t    #      'status', 'satisfaction_matrix']\n2239\t    #\n2240\t    # HOW: parse JSONL, extract the final message, find the embedded\n2241\t    # envelope JSON, INJECT mandatory identity fields ($schema,\n2242\t    # request_id, claim_id, agent_id, role, status) from the known\n2243\t    # ci_executor context (these aren't the agent's job — the agent\n2244\t    # only knows its plan content), synthesize a satisfaction_matrix\n2245\t    # from must_satisfy with verdict=satisfied when the agent omitted\n2246\t    # it, then write the corrected envelope to output_path.\n2247\t    #\n2248\t    # Tier hierarchy: Tier-2 (Make it automatic) — the envelope shape\n2249\t    # is now produced correctly by default; agents don't have to know\n2250\t    # internal kernel identity fields.\n2251\t    if completed.stdout:\n2252\t        envelope = _build_envelope_from_claude_output(\n2253\t            raw_stdout=completed.stdout,\n2254\t            request_id=request_id,\n2255\t            claim_id=claim_id or \"\",\n2256\t            agent_id=agent_id or \"\",\n2257\t            role=role,\n2258\t            subagent_type=subagent_type,\n2259\t            must_satisfy=must_satisfy or [],\n2260\t            dispatch_model=agent_profile.model,\n2261\t        )\n2262\t        envelope[\"details\"][\"agent_contract_hash\"] = agent_contract.contract_hash\n2263\t        output_path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\n2264\t        _write_sanitized_envelope(output_path, envelope)\n2265\t        resolved_transcript_path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\n2266\t        resolved_transcript_path.write_text(completed.stdout, encoding=\"utf-8\")\n2267\t        # Plan ARIA-V3.1-D3 — per-LLM-call cost attribution. Gated on\n2268\t        # _MOCK_MODE_AT_ENTRY frozen sentinel (V3.1-D2) so a mid-run\n2269\t        # CLAUDE_CLI_MOCK flip cannot rewrite mock-mode classification\n2270\t        # between mint + record sites. request_envelope + tools_dir\n2271\t        # MUST both be supplied for the record to fire — None defaults\n2272\t        # preserve V8 backward-compat for callers that haven't migrated."}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"1e3ae1b1-9b85-4c9f-90ed-5bd54ae028a9","uuid":"018f07d7-8cb0-439b-a2ec-1be0f2905de0","timestamp":"2026-09-19T23:22:58.971Z","tool_use_result":{"type":"text","file":{"filePath":"/home/gharunner/actions-runner/_work/aquaculture_platform/aquaculture_platform/aria-worktrees/req-AIR-aria-evidence-judge-0823b429a9e6/tools/aria-poc/ci_executor.py","content":"    # Plan ARIA-V7 §2g v2 + V7.10 envelope-extraction fix.\n    #\n    # WHY: claude -p stream-json emits JSONL events\n    # ({\"result\": \"...\", \"total_cost_usd\": ..., ...}) where the agent's final message may contain the\n    # actual aria/agent-response/v1 envelope as fenced ```json``` block\n    # or as final JSON block. Writing raw JSONL to output_path means\n    # kernel submit-result reads runtime telemetry instead of the envelope,\n    # which fails:\n    #   response_schema: missing required fields:\n    #     ['$schema', 'request_id', 'claim_id', 'agent_id', 'role',\n    #      'status', 'satisfaction_matrix']\n    #\n    # HOW: parse JSONL, extract the final message, find the embedded\n    # envelope JSON, INJECT mandatory identity fields ($schema,\n    # request_id, claim_id, agent_id, role, status) from the known\n    # ci_executor context (these aren't the agent's job — the agent\n    # only knows its plan content), synthesize a satisfaction_matrix\n    # from must_satisfy with verdict=satisfied when the agent omitted\n    # it, then write the corrected envelope to output_path.\n    #\n    # Tier hierarchy: Tier-2 (Make it automatic) — the envelope shape\n    # is now produced correctly by default; agents don't have to know\n    # internal kernel identity fields.\n    if completed.stdout:\n        envelope = _build_envelope_from_claude_output(\n            raw_stdout=completed.stdout,\n            request_id=request_id,\n            claim_id=claim_id or \"\",\n            agent_id=agent_id or \"\",\n            role=role,\n            subagent_type=subagent_type,\n            must_satisfy=must_satisfy or [],\n            dispatch_model=agent_profile.model,\n        )\n        envelope[\"details\"][\"agent_contract_hash\"] = agent_contract.contract_hash\n        output_path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\n        _write_sanitized_envelope(output_path, envelope)\n        resolved_transcript_path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\n        resolved_transcript_path.write_text(completed.stdout, encoding=\"utf-8\")\n        # Plan ARIA-V3.1-D3 — per-LLM-call cost attribution. Gated on\n        # _MOCK_MODE_AT_ENTRY frozen sentinel (V3.1-D2) so a mid-run\n        # CLAUDE_CLI_MOCK flip cannot rewrite mock-mode classification\n        # between mint + record sites. request_envelope + tools_dir\n        # MUST both be supplied for the record to fire — None defaults\n        # preserve V8 backward-compat for callers that haven't migrated.","numLines":45,"startLine":2228,"totalLines":5265}}}

by_key[key]))\n    missing = sorted(set(by_key) - seen)\n    if missing:\n        first = missing[0]\n        raise GovernanceError(f\"batch verdict missing sample item: {first[0]} {first[1]}\")\n\n               run_id=str(item.get(\"run_id\") or \"\"),\n                finding_id=str(item.get(\"finding_id\") or \"\"),\n                verdict=str(verdict.get(\"verdict\") or \"\"),\n                severity=str(verdict.get(\"severity\") or item.get(\"severity\") or \"medium\"),\n                note=str(verdict.get(\"note\") or verdict.get(\"rationale\") or \"batch operator verdict\"),\n                affected_belief_ids=_optional_string_list(verdict.get(\"affected_belief_ids\")),\n                evidence_refs=_optional_string_list(verdict.get(\"evidence_refs\")) or _evidence_refs_from_item(item),\n                rationale=str(verdict.get(\"rationale\") or \"\"),\n                judgment_group_id=str(verdict.get(\"judgment_group_id\") or sample_id),\n                finding_fingerprint=str(item.get(\"finding_fingerprint\") or \"\"),\n                base_dir=base_dir,\n            ),\n        )\n    stored_sample = dict(sample)\n    stored_sample[\"status\"] = \"recorded\"\n    stored_sample[\"recorded_feedback_count\"] = len(rows)\n    return {\"schema_version\": 1, \"sample_id\": sample_id, \"recorded_count\": len(rows), \"feedback\": rows}\n\n\ndef record_ai_feedback_file(\n    *,\n    file_payload: Any,\n    base_dir: str | Path | None = None,\n) -> dict[str, Any]:\n    verdicts = file_payload.get(\"verdicts\") if isinstance(file_payload, dict) else file_payload\n    if not isinstance(verdicts, list):\n        raise GovernanceError(\"AI verdict file must be an array or an object with verdicts array\")\n    rows = []\n    for verdict in verdicts:\n        if not isinstance(verdict, dict):\n            raise GovernanceError(\"AI verdict entries must be JSON objects\")\n        rows.append(\n            record_operator_feedback(\n                tool_id=str(verdict.get(\"tool_id\") or \"\"),\n                run_id=str(verdict.get(\"run_id\") or \"\"),\n                finding_id=str(verdict.get(\"finding_id\") or \"\"),\n                verdict=str(verdict.get(\"verdict\") or \"\"),\n                severity=str(verdict.get(\"severity\") or \"medium\"),\n                note=str(verdict.get(\"note\") or verdict.get(\"rationale\") or \"\"),\n                affected_belief_ids=_optional_string_list(verdict.get(\"affected_belief_ids\")),\n                source_type=\"ai_judge\",\n                judge_id=str(verdict.get(\"judge_id\") or \"\"),\n                model=str(verdict.get(\"model\") or \"\"),\n                prompt_hash=str(verdict.get(\"prompt_hash\") or \"\"),\n                confidence=float(verdict.get(\"confidence\", 0.0)),\n                rationale=str(verdict.get(\"rationale\") or \"\"),\n                evidence_refs=_optional_string_list(verdict.get(\"evidence_refs\")),\n                judgment_group_id=str(verdict.get(\"judgment_group_id\") or \"\"),\n                finding_fingerprint=str(verdict.get(\"finding_fingerprint\") or \"\"),\n                base_dir=base_dir,\n            ),\n        )\n    return {\"schema_version\": 1, \"recorded_count\": len(rows), \"feedback\": rows}\n\n\ndef _has_unverifiable_evidence(rows: list[dict[str, Any]], workspace_root: str | Path) -> bool:\n    \"\"\"Plan 024 §C — True if any judge in the group cites an evidence ref that\n    positively does not resolve in the repo (``missing`` / ``invalid``). A ref\n    that exists but is unverified at a pinned sha (``worktree_candidate``) is\n    given the benefit of the doubt — this gate catches fabricated evidence, not","numLines":100,"startLine":630,"totalLines":1594}}}
